 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# Goal-loop + dual-worktree pipeline (Layers 2+3, v1 plan)
     2	
     3	Status: APPROVED — Gate 1 passed at iteration 4 of 5 (2026-07-18,
     4	codex/gpt-5.5 @ xhigh, `degraded: model-fallback`).
     5	
     6	## Context
     7	
     8	baton v1 (Layer 1, cross-harness usage-limit failover) is complete: Gate-2
     9	passed, a 40-finding surface audit folded, and the live e2e handoff loop
    10	verified through the real installed plugin. Layers 2 and 3 automate the
    11	development methodology itself on top of that foundation: a goal-driven loop
    12	with review gates (Layer 2) and a dual-worktree adversarial write/review
    13	pipeline (Layer 3).
    14	
    15	## Locked decisions (Saksham, 2026-07-18)
    16	
    17	1. **Packaging**: inside the baton repo — new commands `baton loop` and
    18	   `baton pipeline`, sharing the role matrix, resolver, doctor, and
    19	   checkpoint/handoff machinery.
    20	2. **Layer 3 swap cadence**: per subtask — writer and reviewer swap seats
    21	   every subtask; the planner role plans and the merger role performs the
    22	   final adversarial merge (concrete chains live in `baton.config.json`).
    23	3. **Layer 2 smoke gate**: one staged gate after planning — the loop must
    24	   produce a minimal end-to-end slice (build + run + one real assertion) and
    25	   get explicit approval before the full run; the 5-iteration cap governs
    26	   every gate.
    27	4. **Driver**: a supervisor CLI — spawns headless role children
    28	   (`claude -p` / `codex exec`), parses verdicts, and invokes baton handoff
    29	   automatically on limit death. Survives the operator's session ending.
    30	
    31	## Design
    32	
    33	### Loop spec
    34	
    35	`loop.json` at repo root (schema `baton/loop@1`), scaffolded by
    36	`baton loop init "<goal>"`:
    37	
    38	- `goal`, `constraints[]` (falsifiable acceptance criteria — the loop's exit
    39	  condition), `smoke` ({cmd, expect} — the staged-gate slice definition),
    40	- `phases` — an ordered list of `{id, role}` entries: `id` is a free label
    41	  for progress display; `role` MUST be a matrix role ID (validation fails on
    42	  any unknown role before a child spawns). Default:
    43	  plan(`planner`) → gate-1(`plan-reviewer`) → smoke — itself a TDD mini-cycle
    44	  per the repo contract: smoke-tests(`test-author`) →
    45	  smoke-verify(`test-verifier`) → smoke-build(`implementer`) → the human
    46	  smoke gate — → per-subtask cycle (`test-author` → `test-verifier` →
    47	  `implementer` → `subtask-reviewer`) → gate-2(`final-reviewer-a`,
    48	  `final-reviewer-b`) → done,
    49	- `budgets` ({iterationCap: 5, perRoleTimeoutMin, maxChildrenPerPhase}),
    50	- roles resolve through the existing `baton.config.json` matrix — never
    51	  hardcoded models.
    52	
    53	### Role-matrix additions (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 1)
    54	
    55	The loop references roles by ID only; every referenced role must exist in the
    56	matrix or `loop.json` validation fails (`unknown role '<id>'`, exit 2, before
    57	any child spawns). Four roles are ADDED to `baton.config.json` +
    58	`templates/baton.config.json.tpl` in the first implementation task:
    59	
    60	- `subtask-reviewer` — per-subtask diff review inside the loop cycle.
    61	- `merger` — the only role that writes main in pipeline mode; adversarial
    62	  re-check before merge.
    63	- `worker-a`, `worker-b` — the two pipeline seats. Subtask N assigns
    64	  writer = seat (N mod 2), reviewer = the other seat; the SEATS swap, and each
    65	  seat's concrete platform/model comes from its configured chain via the
    66	  resolver. No vendor or model name appears in this plan, child prompts, or
    67	  loop code — chains live in config only.
    68	
    69	### `baton loop` (Layer 2)
    70	
    71	- `baton loop init <goal>` — scaffold spec + plan template; idempotent,
    72	  dry-run-able, same two-phase plan/apply discipline as `baton init`.
    73	- `baton loop run [--approve-smoke <token>]` — the state machine. State lives
    74	  in gitignored `.handoff/loop/state.json` + `journal.ndjson` (same atomic
    75	  write + journal replay machinery as the bundle; loop position is also
    76	  checkpointed into the handoff bundle so a limit death carries it).
    77	
    78	### Root + ownership discipline (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 2)
    79	
    80	One authoritative root: the MAIN repo root where `loop.json` lives. The
    81	supervisor is the SOLE writer of `.handoff/loop/` and of the main root's
    82	handoff bundle; every supervisor-driven baton invocation passes explicit
    83	`--root <mainRoot>` and a supervisor-owned stable session hint
    84	(`--session loop-<runId>` — a NEW flag added to `checkpoint`; `receive`
    85	already has it), so the bundle has exactly one stable owner.
    86	
    87	**Child-hook policy (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 1; iteration 3,
    88	finding 1)** — children never touch OR read any bundle, by construction: the
    89	supervisor sets `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD=1` in every child's environment, and
    90	EVERY baton hook command — the hook entrypoints, `baton checkpoint`, and
    91	`baton session-start` (which would otherwise read the bundle and inject
    92	pending-handoff resume context into the child) — quietly no-ops (exit 0,
    93	empty output) when it is set. This covers
    94	Layer-2 children running in the MAIN root (where root discovery would
    95	otherwise find the supervisor-owned bundle and the foreign-session guard
    96	would reject their stable hints) and pipeline children in worktrees alike;
    97	narrative checkpointing for supervised work is the supervisor's job, driven
    98	by parsed child results. Contract tests: (a) Layer-2 — two concurrent
    99	main-root children with distinct stable session hints and live hooks →
   100	zero bundle mutations, zero foreign-session rejections, supervisor
   101	`--session` checkpoints land; (b) pipeline — same from two worktrees while
   102	the supervisor writes loop state.
   103	- Each role invocation is a headless child under the supervisor:
   104	  - claude-code roles: `claude -p "<role prompt>" --permission-mode
   105	    acceptEdits --allowedTools ...` (allowlist per role: reviewers read-only).
   106	  - codex roles: `codex exec -C <root> -s <sandbox> --model <m> -c
   107	    model_reasoning_effort=<e> "<prompt>" </dev/null` (stdin closed — the
   108	    stdin-hang class is a known field failure).
   109	  - Verdict contract: children end with the structured tail
   110	    (`VERDICT: APPROVED|APPROVED_WITH_NOTES|BLOCKED` + FINDINGS) already used
   111	    by every review gate; the supervisor parses exactly that.
   112	
   113	### Child supervision contract (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 7)
   114	
   115	`children.mjs` owns the full child lifecycle, not just argv assembly:
   116	
   117	- Spawn detached in its OWN process group; stdin closed at spawn (the
   118	  codex-exec stdin-hang class); per-role timeout → SIGTERM to the group, 10 s
   119	  grace, then SIGKILL to the group (no zombie grandchildren; reap verified in
   120	  tests with a child that forks a sleeper).
   121	- Full child output streams to `.handoff/loop/children/<child-id>.log` with a
   122	  byte cap (head preserved, middle truncated with a marker — the verdict tail
   123	  is at the END so the tail is never truncated).
   124	- **Verdict parsing is region-bounded (prompt-echo defense)**: only the
   125	  child's FINAL message region is parsed — claude-code children run with a
   126	  structured output format and the parser reads the result field; codex
   127	  children are parsed strictly AFTER the last `tokens used` marker. The
   128	  prompt region (which quotes `VERDICT:` in its instructions) is never
   129	  scanned. Multiple tails in the final region → the LAST one wins.
   130	- An unparseable, absent, or truncated verdict is BLOCKED-with-escalation,
   131	  never APPROVED; a child that exits after making edits but before a verdict
   132	  parks the subtask with its worktree preserved for inspection.
   133	- A generic nonzero exit classifies as `other-error` (existing classifier
   134	  rule) → the loop retries once, then parks; it NEVER triggers limit failover
   135	  without limit-specific evidence.
   136	- Fixture-driven tests: prompt echo, multiple verdict tails, truncated
   137	  transcript, timeout kill/reap, zombie grandchild, non-limit nonzero exit.
   138	
   139	### Supervisor lifetime and recovery (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 5)
   140	
   141	"Survives the operator's session ending" is a defined mechanism, not a hope:
   142	
   143	- `baton loop run` is FOREGROUND by default. `--detach` re-launches the
   144	  supervisor in its own session (`setsid`), output to
   145	  `.handoff/loop/supervisor.log`, and returns.
   146	- A run lock `.handoff/loop/supervisor.lock` carries {pid, process
   147	  start-time, host, runId} — the same provably-dead machinery as the
   148	  Layer-1 bundle lock. A second `loop run` against a LIVE supervisor is
   149	  refused; against a provably-dead one it recovers.
   150	- The state machine is journal-replayable: recovery replays
   151	  `journal.ndjson`, marks any in-flight child of the dead supervisor as
   152	  interrupted (its process group is gone or killed on adoption), and either
   153	  continues from the last completed step or parks with an escalation note —
   154	  never re-runs a completed gate iteration.
   155	- Acceptance adds a supervisor-death test: kill the supervisor (and its
   156	  launching session) mid-subtask → a fresh `loop run` recovers via the
   157	  journal and continues or parks; no orphaned children survive (process
   158	  groups reaped on adoption).
   159	
   160	- **Smoke gate (staleness-proof — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 8; TDD order —
   161	  iteration 2, finding 2)**: after gate-1, the smoke slice is built as a TDD
   162	  mini-cycle (test-author writes the failing smoke assertion → test-verifier
   163	  approves it → implementer writes only enough to pass it — the repo TDD
   164	  contract applies to the slice, not just to subtasks); the supervisor then
   165	  runs `smoke.cmd`, records output, and STOPS, writing
   166	  `.handoff/loop/SMOKE-REVIEW.md` plus `smoke-approval.json` containing an
   167	  approval token = digest over {loop-state digest, smoke.cmd, output digest,
   168	  HEAD, content-sensitive git digest (existing snapshot machinery), child
   169	  assignment}. Resuming requires `baton loop run --approve-smoke <token>`;
   170	  the supervisor recomputes the digest first and REFUSES a drifted token
   171	  (anything changed since the human looked) — it parks for a fresh smoke run
   172	  instead. No full-run budget is spent before a valid approval.
   173	- **Limit failover (the baton integration — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 3)**:
   174	  an exact transaction, not a loose chain. On child death: (1) reap the
   175	  process group and freeze the transcript; (2) classify via `baton detect`;
   176	  non-limit → the retry/park path above. On a limit classification:
   177	  (3) checkpoint the loop position INTO THE BUNDLE (supervisor
   178	  `checkpoint --session loop-<runId>`) so the seal carries it and the receipt
   179	  token binds it; (4) seal via `finalize --reason-class usage-limit --root
   180	  <mainRoot>` (an already-dead/unsealed bundle takes the degraded-open path
   181	  receive already supports); (5) refresh probes; (6) `receive --prepare` then
   182	  `--commit` back-to-back with IDENTICAL intake flags, the supervisor session
   183	  hint, and the same explicit root, with NO intervening writes; (7) resolver
   184	  output (with the dead entry avoided) picks the relaunch platform+model;
   185	  (8) relaunch the child with the resume prompt. A stale-token rejection
   186	  triggers ONE automatic re-prepare/commit retry; a second failure parks.
   187	  Between prepare and commit the failover holds the loop transaction lock and
   188	  writes NOTHING — `.handoff/loop/` writes are not token-bound inputs, so
   189	  this is enforced by construction and ASSERTED in tests (fs spy: zero
   190	  `.handoff/loop/` writes inside the window), not assumed. Drift-rejection
   191	  tests inject the inputs the token actually binds: git change and probe
   192	  refresh between prepare and commit → stale-token → one retry path. Plus:
   193	  open-bundle (unsealed) limit death; the full seal→receive→relaunch happy
   194	  path.
   195	- **Model-level failover (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 4)**: the resolver
   196	  gains entry-level avoidance — `avoidEntries: [{platform, model}]` alongside
   197	  the existing platform-level `avoid[]` — and the classifier gains a
   198	  `model-unavailable` signature class (distinct from usage-limit; seeded with
   199	  the verified account-tier rejection string). A model rejected at spawn adds
   200	  its {platform, model} entry to avoidEntries and re-resolves: other models
   201	  on the SAME platform remain selectable. Named test: first chain entry
   202	  rejected as model-unavailable → the next entry on the same platform is
   203	  selected, with no platform-wide avoidance.
   204	  All platforms/entries exhausted → the loop parks with a resume-at hint
   205	  instead of dying.
   206	- **Caps**: every gate loops at most 5 iterations; on exhaustion the loop
   207	  parks and escalates (writes `.handoff/loop/ESCALATION.md`, notifies) — never
   208	  a 6th run.
   209	
   210	### `baton pipeline` (Layer 3 — a loop preset)
   211	
   212	- `baton pipeline run` = `loop run` with the dual-worktree subtask cycle:
   213	  - Two persistent worktrees `.worktrees/wt-a`, `.worktrees/wt-b`
   214	    (`git worktree add`); `pipeline init` ensure-lines `.worktrees/` into
   215	    `.gitignore` (same idempotent machinery as the `.handoff/` line).
   216	  - Subtask N: the writer seat implements on a namespaced branch
   217	    (`baton/wt-<seat>/subtask-N`) in its worktree; the reviewer seat reviews
   218	    the diff from the OTHER worktree (fresh checkout, fresh context). Seats
   219	    swap every subtask: worker-a writes / worker-b reviews → worker-b writes /
   220	    worker-a reviews → … (concrete models per seat come from the role
   221	    matrix — see Role-matrix additions).
   222	  - Verdict flow: APPROVED → the merger role adversarially re-checks and
   223	    merges into main (the ONLY writer to main); BLOCKED → writer fixes,
   224	    ≤5 iterations, then escalate. After each merge both worktrees
   225	    fast-forward.
   226	
   227	  **Worktree transaction layer (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 5)** —
   228	  `worktrees.mjs` treats every git operation as a guarded transaction:
   229	  - Branch namespace `baton/wt-*/…` is loop-owned; anything else is never
   230	    touched.
   231	  - Preflight before each child: worktree exists in `git worktree list`,
   232	    index clean, HEAD on the expected branch — a dirty or drifted worktree
   233	    parks the subtask instead of spawning.
   234	  - Postflight after each child: HEAD still on the expected branch, main
   235	    unmoved by the child — violation parks with the evidence.
   236	  - Merges run under a supervisor-held merge lock; worktree sync after merge
   237	    is ff-only (a non-ff sync is a corruption signal → park); merge conflicts
   238	    park + escalate, never auto-resolved.
   239	  - Stale metadata self-heals: a raw-deleted worktree directory is detected
   240	    (`git worktree list` vs fs), pruned (`git worktree prune`), and re-added;
   241	    teardown uses `git worktree remove` + branch deletion so acceptance
   242	    constraint 5 (no residue) holds.
   243	  - Adversarial tests: stale branch from a previous run, dirty worker,
   244	    main moved mid-subtask, merge conflict, raw-deleted `.worktrees/`,
   245	    untracked `.worktrees/` (missing gitignore line → doctor check).
   246	
   247	  **Attribution enforcement for child commits (Gate-1 iteration 1,
   248	  finding 6)** — the invariant is enforced, not assumed:
   249	  - The supervisor sets `GIT_AUTHOR_NAME/EMAIL` and `GIT_COMMITTER_NAME/EMAIL`
   250	    (sole author identity) in EVERY child's environment.
   251	  - Every merge is gated on an attribution scan over the exact range
   252	    `main..baton/wt-<seat>/subtask-N` — author/committer identity plus the
   253	    forbidden-trailer patterns (the existing doctor patterns, applied to the
   254	    full range, not a last-50 window). A violation blocks the merge and
   255	    parks.
   256	  - The existing doctor 50-commit scan remains the repo-wide backstop.
   257	
   258	### Module layout (all under `core/src/loop/`)
   259	
   260	`spec.mjs` (load/validate loop.json incl. role-existence check) · `state.mjs`
   261	(state machine + journal + smoke-approval token) · `children.mjs` (full child
   262	lifecycle: spawn/process-group/timeout/log-cap, region-bounded verdict parse,
   263	detect-on-exit) · `failover.mjs` (the limit-death transaction) ·
   264	`worktrees.mjs` (guarded git transactions, branch discipline, attribution
   265	range scan) · `commands/loop.mjs`, `commands/pipeline.mjs`. Plus three Layer-1
   266	extensions: `roles/resolve.mjs` gains `avoidEntries[]` (entry-level
   267	avoidance, backward-compatible — omitted means current behavior); the
   268	signature table gains the `model-unavailable` class; `checkpoint` gains a
   269	`--session <hint>` flag (supervisor identity), and the
   270	`BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD` no-op guard lands in the hook entrypoints,
   271	`checkpoint`, AND `session-start` (every hook-invoked command).
   272	
   273	### Acceptance constraints (the loop's exit condition)
   274	
   275	1. On a fixture repo, `baton loop run` drives a toy goal end-to-end headlessly:
   276	   plan → gate-1 → smoke slice AS A TDD MINI-CYCLE (failing smoke assertion
   277	   authored and verifier-approved BEFORE the slice implementation — order
   278	   asserted from the journal) → parked for approval → (approved) → 2 subtasks
   279	   TDD → gate-2 → done, with review artifacts under `reviews/` in the fixture.
   280	   Layer-2 children run in the main root with live hooks and neither mutate
   281	   NOR read the supervisor-owned bundle: under `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD=1`,
   282	   hook commands on all three platforms produce zero bundle writes AND zero
   283	   session-start resume context (both asserted).
   284	2. Simulated limit death (child exits with a verbatim limit banner) mid-subtask
   285	   → the loop seals, remaps the role off the dead platform, resumes on the
   286	   other harness, and completes the subtask. Chained A→B→A tested. Separately:
   287	   a model-unavailable rejection at spawn selects the NEXT entry on the same
   288	   platform (no platform-wide avoidance).
   289	3. `baton pipeline run` completes 2 subtasks with alternating writer/reviewer
   290	   across the two worktrees; the attribution range scan passes for main AND
   291	   both worker branch ranges (child commits included) — sole author, zero AI
   292	   attribution; reviewer children run read-only.
   293	4. The 5-cap parks + escalates on every gate (unit-forced exhaustion test);
   294	   no gate ever runs a 6th iteration. Killing the supervisor (and its
   295	   launching session) mid-subtask → a fresh `loop run` recovers via the
   296	   journal and continues or parks, with no orphaned children.
   297	5. Deleting `.handoff/` and `.worktrees/` leaves the fixture repo exactly as
   298	   a plain git repo (no residue outside gitignored trees).
   299	6. All existing 878 tests stay green; the new suite covers spec validation,
   300	   state-machine transitions (unit, injected io), child verdict parsing
   301	   (fixture transcripts), failover (fake children), and worktree discipline.
   302	
   303	### Top risks
   304	
   305	1. **Headless permission surfaces** — Claude Code children need permission
   306	   flags that stay inside the sandboxed repo; reviewers get read-only
   307	   allowlists. Mitigation: per-role allowlist table + spike task 0.
   308	2. **Verdict-parse drift** — models sometimes wrap the tail; the
   309	   region-bounded parser (Child supervision contract) takes the LAST tail in
   310	   the final-message region only and treats an unparseable one as
   311	   BLOCKED-with-escalation, never as APPROVED.
   312	3. **Cost runaway** — per-phase child caps + iteration caps + the smoke gate
   313	   before any full-run spend; every child's token/duration logged to the loop
   314	   journal.
   315	4. **Worktree state divergence** — only the merger writes main; workers are
   316	   branch-jailed; ff-sync after each merge; `baton pipeline doctor` checks
   317	   worktree health.
   318	5. **Cross-platform child API drift** — the spawn contract lives in ONE module
   319	   (children.mjs) with per-platform fixtures, same seam discipline as
   320	   normalize.mjs.
   321	
   322	## Out of scope for this milestone
   323	
   324	Multi-repo loops; parallel subtask fan-out (>1 writer at once); cloud/cron
   325	driving (the supervisor is local-first; scheduled runs are a later layer);
   326	LLM-judged smoke verdicts (the smoke gate is a human gate by decision 3).
   327

/bin/zsh -lc 'nl -ba tests/helpers/fakeio.mjs' in /Users/saksham/baton
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	// Fake `io` object matching the injected-io contract from docs/design/core.md:
     2	// { cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now }. Backed by memfs.
     3	// stdout/stderr collect writes; io.stdoutText()/io.stderrText() flatten them.
     4	// now() returns a settable ISO string (io.setNow). execFile resolves from an
     5	// execResults map keyed by the joined command string.
     6	//
     7	// Wave-B2 additions (bundle lock layer) — all optional, defaulted, and purely
     8	// additive so no prior consumer is affected:
     9	//   host / pid / startTime  — the process-identity facts the lock model writes
    10	//     into owner.json and compares on contention (see plan §Concurrency, Lock
    11	//     model iterations 3+4).
    12	//   processAlive(pid, startTime) — the "provably dead" oracle. Default: only
    13	//     THIS io's own {pid, startTime} is alive; every other descriptor reads as
    14	//     dead. Override per test to model a live foreign owner or a pid/start-time
    15	//     mismatch (reused pid).
    16	//   newFencingToken() — the injected per-acquisition token source. Deterministic
    17	//     monotonic counter ("tok-1", "tok-2", …), matching the injected-io
    18	//     discipline already used for now()/execFile so lock acquisition is
    19	//     reproducible in tests rather than drawing from Math.random.
    20	
    21	import { makeMemfs } from './memfs.mjs';
    22	
    23	export function makeIo({
    24	  files = {},
    25	  env = {},
    26	  stdin = '',
    27	  execResults = {},
    28	  now = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z',
    29	  host = 'host-A',
    30	  pid = 4242,
    31	  startTime = 111000,
    32	  processAlive = null,
    33	  fencingSeed = 1,
    34	  execPath = process.execPath,
    35	} = {}) {
    36	  const { fs, files: filesSnapshot } = makeMemfs(files);
    37	  const outChunks = [];
    38	  const errChunks = [];
    39	  let currentNow = now;
    40	  let tokenCounter = fencingSeed - 1;
    41	  const defaultAlive = (p, s) => p === pid && s === startTime;
    42	
    43	  const stdout = { write: (s) => { outChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
    44	  const stderr = { write: (s) => { errChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
    45	
    46	  const execFile = (cmd, args = [], /* opts */ _opts = {}) => {
    47	    const key = [cmd, ...(Array.isArray(args) ? args : [])].join(' ');
    48	    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    49	      if (!(key in execResults)) {
    50	        const e = new Error(`spawn ${cmd} ENOENT`);
    51	        e.code = 'ENOENT';
    52	        return reject(e);
    53	      }
    54	      const r = execResults[key];
    55	      const code = r.code ?? 0;
    56	      const out = r.stdout ?? '';
    57	      const err = r.stderr ?? '';
    58	      if (code !== 0) {
    59	        // util.promisify(execFile) rejects on nonzero exit; error carries code + streams.
    60	        const e = new Error(`Command failed: ${key}`);
    61	        e.code = code;
    62	        e.stdout = out;
    63	        e.stderr = err;
    64	        return reject(e);
    65	      }
    66	      resolve({ stdout: out, stderr: err });
    67	    });
    68	  };
    69	
    70	  return {
    71	    cwd: '/repo',
    72	    env,
    73	    stdin,
    74	    stdout,
    75	    stderr,
    76	    fs,
    77	    execFile,
    78	    now: () => currentNow,
    79	    setNow: (iso) => { currentNow = iso; },
    80	    execPath,
    81	    host,
    82	    pid,
    83	    startTime,
    84	    processAlive: processAlive || defaultAlive,
    85	    // Tiny retry budget: unit tests exercising live-lock refusal stay fast
    86	    // while the retry code path itself still runs (production default: 120x25ms).
    87	    lockRetry: { attempts: 2, delayMs: 2 },
    88	    newFencingToken: () => `tok-${++tokenCounter}`,
    89	    stdoutText: () => outChunks.join(''),
    90	    stderrText: () => errChunks.join(''),
    91	    files: filesSnapshot,
    92	  };
    93	}

   176	    76	const REC = (sha, an, ae, cn, ce, body) => [sha, an, ae, cn, ce, body].join('\x00');
   177	    77	const logStdout = (...recs) => recs.map((r) => r + '\x1e').join('');
   178	    78	const CLEAN_LOG = logStdout(REC('c1', NAME, EMAIL, NAME, EMAIL, 'subtask work'));
   179	    79	
   180	    80	/**
   181	    81	 * A STRICT, stateful fake git: unstubbed commands reject; worktree add/list and
   182	    82	 * checkout -b are modeled so setup + preflight/postflight + merge cohere.
   183	    83	 * `over` overrides specific responses (e.g. a merge conflict).
   184	    84	 */
   185	    85	function pipelineGit({ over = {}, log = CLEAN_LOG } = {}) {
   186	    86	  const added = /** @type {Map<string,string>} */ (new Map()); // path -> branch
   187	    87	  const branchAt = /** @type {Map<string,string>} */ (new Map()); // cwd -> current branch
   188	    88	  const calls = /** @type {any[]} */ ([]);
   189	    89	  const lockProbeRef = { fn: /** @type {null | (() => boolean)} */ (null) };
   190	    90	
   191	    91	  const list = () => {
   192	    92	    const entries = [{ path: '/repo', branch: 'main' }];
   193	    93	    for (const [path, branch] of added) entries.push({ path, branch: branchAt.get(path) ?? branch });
   194	    94	    return entries.map((e) => `worktree ${e.path}\nHEAD ${MAIN_SHA}\nbranch refs/heads/${e.branch}\n`).join('\n');
   195	    95	  };
   196	    96	
   197	    97	  const fn = (/** @type {string} */ cmd, /** @type {string[]} */ args = [], /** @type {any} */ opts = {}) => {
   198	    98	    const argstr = args.join(' ');
   199	    99	    calls.push({ cmd, args, argstr, cwd: opts?.cwd, lockPresent: lockProbeRef.fn ? lockProbeRef.fn() : undefined });
   200	   100	    if (cmd !== 'git') return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error('spawn ENOENT'), { code: 'ENOENT' }));
   201	   101	
   202	   102	    // Explicit overrides win.
   203	   103	    const okey = Object.keys(over).sort((a, b) => b.length - a.length).find((k) => argstr.includes(k));
   204	   104	    if (okey) {
   205	   105	      const r = over[okey];
   206	   106	      if (r?.reject) return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error(r.stderr || 'git failed'), { code: r.code ?? 1, stdout: r.stdout ?? '', stderr: r.stderr ?? '' }));
   207	   107	      return Promise.resolve({ stdout: r?.stdout ?? '', stderr: r?.stderr ?? '' });
   208	   108	    }
   209	   109	
   210	   110	    if (argstr.startsWith('worktree list')) return Promise.resolve({ stdout: list(), stderr: '' });
   211	   111	    if (argstr.startsWith('worktree add')) {
   212	   112	      const path = args.find((a) => a.startsWith('/repo/.worktrees/'));
   213	   113	      const bi = args.indexOf('-b');
   214	   114	      const branch = bi >= 0 ? args[bi + 1] : 'baton/wt-x/base';
   215	   115	      if (path) { added.set(path, branch); branchAt.set(path, branch); }
   216	   116	      return Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' });
   217	   117	    }
   218	   118	    if (argstr.startsWith('worktree prune') || argstr.startsWith('worktree remove')) return Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' });
   219	   119	    if (argstr.startsWith('checkout -b')) { if (opts?.cwd) branchAt.set(opts.cwd, args[args.indexOf('-b') + 1]); return Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' }); }
   220	   120	    if (argstr.startsWith('status --porcelain')) return Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' });
   221	   121	    if (argstr === 'rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD') return Promise.resolve({ stdout: `${branchAt.get(opts?.cwd) ?? 'main'}\n`, stderr: '' });
   222	   122	    if (argstr.startsWith('rev-parse')) return Promise.resolve({ stdout: `${MAIN_SHA}\n`, stderr: '' });
   223	   123	    if (argstr.startsWith('log ')) return Promise.resolve({ stdout: log, stderr: '' });
   224	   124	    if (argstr.startsWith('merge')) return Promise.resolve({ stdout: 'Fast-forward', stderr: '' });
   225	   125	    if (argstr.startsWith('branch -D')) return Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' });
   226	   126	    return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error(`unstubbed git: ${argstr}`), { code: 'ENOSTUB' }));
   227	   127	  };
   228	   128	  fn.calls = calls;
   229	   129	  fn.git = () => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git');
   230	   130	  fn.issued = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re) => calls.some((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr));
   231	   131	  fn.matching = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re) => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr));
   232	   132	  fn.withCwd = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re, /** @type {string} */ cwd) => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr) && c.cwd === cwd);
   233	   133	  fn.setLockProbe = (/** @type {() => boolean} */ f) => { lockProbeRef.fn = f; };
   234	   134	  return fn;
   235	   135	}
   236	   136	
   237	   137	function fakeRunner(io, script) {
   238	   138	  const queue = [...script];
   239	   139	  const calls = /** @type {any[]} */ ([]);
   240	   140	  const fn = (/** @type {any} */ spec, /** @type {any} */ opts = {}) => {
   241	   141	    calls.push({ spec, opts, command: spec?.command, args: spec?.args ?? [], env: spec?.env });
   242	   142	    const r = queue.shift() ?? { verdict: 'APPROVED' };
   243	   143	    if (typeof r.logContent === 'string' && opts.logPath) {
   244	   144	      const dir = opts.logPath.slice(0, opts.logPath.lastIndexOf('/'));
   245	   145	      io.fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
   246	   146	      io.fs.writeFileSync(opts.logPath, r.logContent);
   247	   147	    }
   248	   148	    return Promise.resolve({ timedOut: false, exitCode: r.exitCode ?? 0, verdict: r.verdict ?? 'APPROVED', findings: r.findings ?? '', logPath: opts.logPath });
   249	   149	  };
   250	   150	  fn.calls = calls;
   251	   151	  return fn;
   252	   152	}
   253	   153	
   254	   154	function makePipeRepo({ spec = pipelineSpec(), config = PIPELINE_CONFIG, files = {}, git, runner, env = {} } = {}) {
   255	   155	  const io = makeIo({
   256	   156	    now: T0,
   257	   157	    host: 'pipe-host',
   258	   158	    pid: 4242,
   259	   159	    startTime: 111000,
   260	   160	    env,
   261	   161	    files: {
   262	   162	      [SIG_ABS]: SIG_CONTENT,
   263	   163	      '/repo/.git/HEAD': 'ref: refs/heads/main\n',
   264	   164	      '/repo/baton.config.json': JSON.stringify(config, null, 2),
   265	   165	      ...(spec ? { '/repo/loop.json': JSON.stringify(spec, null, 2) + '\n' } : {}),
   266	   166	      ...files,
   267	   167	    },
   268	   168	  });
   269	   169	  const g = git ?? pipelineGit();
   270	   170	  g.setLockProbe(() => io.fs.existsSync(MERGE_LOCK));
   271	   171	  io.execFile = g;
   272	   172	  const fake = runner ?? fakeRunner(io, []);
   273	   173	  io.superviseChild = fake;
   274	   174	  io.__git = g;
   275	   175	  io.__runner = fake;
   276	   176	  return io;
   277	   177	}
   278	   178	
   279	   179	// A full clean subtask = writer APPROVED, reviewer APPROVED, merger APPROVED.
   280	   180	const cleanSubtask = () => [{ verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'APPROVED' }];
   281	   181	const argsOf = (call) => (call?.args ?? []).map(String);
   282	   182	const valAfter = (arr, flag) => { const i = arr.indexOf(flag); return i >= 0 ? arr[i + 1] : undefined; };
   283	   183	const childWithModel = (runner, model) => runner.calls.filter((c) => argsOf(c).includes(model));
   284	   184	
   285	   185	// ===========================================================================
   286	   186	describe('pipeline — CLI routing & flags', () => {
   287	   187	  it('no subcommand -> usage error (exit 2) naming a subcommand, not "unknown command"', async () => {
   288	   188	    const io = makePipeRepo();
   289	   189	    const code = await run(['pipeline'], io);
   290	   190	    assert.equal(code, 2);
   291	   191	    const out = io.stderrText() + io.stdoutText();
   292	   192	    assert.match(out, /subcommand|run/i, 'the error guides toward a subcommand');
   293	   193	    assert.doesNotMatch(out, /unknown command/i, 'pipeline IS a known command');
   294	   194	  });
   295	   195	
   296	   196	  it('an unknown subcommand exits 2 naming it', async () => {
   297	   197	    const io = makePipeRepo();
   298	   198	    const code = await run(['pipeline', 'frobnicate'], io);
   299	   199	    assert.equal(code, 2);
   300	   200	    assert.match(io.stderrText() + io.stdoutText(), /frobnicate/, 'the error names the bad subcommand');
   301	   201	  });
   302	   202	
   303	   203	  it('a stray positional / unknown flag exits 2', async () => {
   304	   204	    assert.equal(await run(['pipeline', 'run', 'stray'], makePipeRepo()), 2);
   305	   205	    assert.equal(await run(['pipeline', 'run', '--no-such-flag'], makePipeRepo()), 2);
   306	   206	  });
   307	   207	
   308	   208	  it('BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD does NOT suppress pipeline run (supervisor-side)', async () => {
   309	   209	    const io = makePipeRepo({ env: { BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD: '1' }, runner: undefined });
   310	   210	    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [...cleanSubtask(), ...cleanSubtask()]);
   311	   211	    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
   312	   212	    const code = await run(['pipeline', 'run'], io);
   313	   213	    assert.equal(code, 0, 'the guard is child-only; the supervisor command still runs');
   314	   214	    assert.ok(io.__runner.calls.length >= 1, 'children spawned under the guard env');
   315	   215	  });
   316	   216	});
   317	   217	
   318	   218	// ===========================================================================
   319	   219	describe('pipeline — preconditions', () => {
   320	   220	  it('no loop.json -> exit 2 naming loop.json, zero spawns', async () => {
   321	   221	    const io = makePipeRepo({ spec: null });
   322	   222	    const code = await run(['pipeline', 'run'], io);
   323	   223	    assert.equal(code, 2);
   324	   224	    const out = io.stderrText() + io.stdoutText();
   325	   225	    assert.match(out, /loop\.json/i, 'the error names the missing spec file');
   326	   226	    assert.doesNotMatch(out, /unknown command/i, 'pipeline is routed — the error is about the missing spec, not the command');
   327	   227	    assert.equal(io.__runner.calls.length, 0, 'no child spawned');
   328	   228	  });

   334	   234	      assert.equal(code, 2, 'a pipeline needs subtasks');
   335	   235	      assert.match(io.stderrText() + io.stdoutText(), /subtasks/i, 'the error names subtasks');
   336	   236	      assert.equal(io.__runner.calls.length, 0, 'no child spawned without subtasks');

   348	   248	  });
   349	   249	});

   352	   252	describe('pipeline — seat alternation (writer)', () => {
   353	   253	  it('subtask 1 writes in wt-a on baton/wt-a/subtask-t1 (worker-a model); subtask 2 writes in wt-b on baton/wt-b/subtask-t2 (worker-b model)', async () => {
   354	   254	    const io = makePipeRepo({ runner: undefined });
   355	   255	    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [...cleanSubtask(), ...cleanSubtask()]);
   356	   256	    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
   357	   257	    const code = await run(['pipeline', 'run'], io);
   358	   258	    assert.equal(code, 0);
   359	   259

   361	   261	    assert.equal(io.__git.withCwd(/^checkout -b baton\/wt-a\/subtask-t1$/, WT_A).length, 1, 'subtask 1 branch created in wt-a');
   362	   262	    assert.equal(io.__git.withCwd(/^checkout -b baton\/wt-b\/subtask-t2$/, WT_B).length, 1, 'subtask 2 branch created in wt-b');
   363	   263	
   364	   264	    // Writer children resolve from the per-seat worker chains — models differ.
   365	   265	    const w1 = childWithModel(io.__runner, 'wa-model');
   366	   266	    const w2 = childWithModel(io.__runner, 'wb-model');
   367	   267	    assert.ok(w1.length >= 1, 'subtask 1 writer uses the worker-a model');
   368	   268	    assert.ok(w2.length >= 1, 'subtask 2 writer uses the worker-b model');
   369	   269	    assert.equal(valAfter(argsOf(w1[0]), '-C'), WT_A, 'subtask 1 writer works in the wt-a worktree');
   370	   270	    assert.equal(valAfter(argsOf(w2[0]), '-C'), WT_B, 'subtask 2 writer works in the wt-b worktree');
   371	   271	    assert.equal(valAfter(argsOf(w1[0]), '-s'), 'workspace-write', 'the writer may edit');
   372	   272	  });
   373	   273	});

   393	   293	describe('pipeline — verdict flow', () => {
   394	   294	  it('reviewer APPROVED -> the MERGER child runs (merger model); on its APPROVED the SUPERVISOR merges via mergeSubtask', async () => {
   395	   295	    const io = makePipeRepo({ runner: undefined });
   396	   296	    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [...cleanSubtask(), ...cleanSubtask()]);

   402	   302	
   403	   303	    // The REAL merge happened through mergeSubtask: merge at /repo under the lock
   404	   304	    // + two ff-only main syncs (one per seat). The merger CHILD issues no git
   405	   305	    // (children run via the fake runner), so the merge is the supervisor's.
   406	   306	    const m1 = io.__git.matching(/^merge baton\/wt-a\/subtask-t1$/);
   407	   307	    assert.equal(m1.length, 1, 'the subtask-1 branch is merged exactly once');
   408	   308	    assert.equal(m1[0].cwd, '/repo', 'the merge into main runs at the repo root');
   409	   309	    assert.equal(m1[0].lockPresent, true, 'the merge ran under the merge lock');
   410	   310	    assert.equal(io.__git.matching(/^merge --ff-only main$/).length >= 2, true, 'both worktrees fast-forward to main after each merge');
   411	   311	  });
   412	   312	
   413	   313	  it('reviewer BLOCKED -> the SAME writer seat re-runs with the findings in its prompt', async () => {
   414	   314	    const io = makePipeRepo({ runner: undefined });
   415	   315	    // subtask 1: writer APPROVED, reviewer BLOCKED (findings), then writer re-run APPROVED, reviewer APPROVED, merger APPROVED; then subtask 2 clean.
   416	   316	    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [
   417	   317	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, // writer 1
   418	   318	      { verdict: 'BLOCKED', findings: 'REVIEW-FINDING-X' }, // reviewer 1
   419	   319	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, // writer 1 re-run
   420	   320	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, // reviewer 1 again
   421	   321	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, // merger 1
   422	   322	      ...cleanSubtask(), // subtask 2
   423	   323	    ]);
   424	   324	    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
   425	   325	    await run(['pipeline', 'run'], io);
   426	   326	    // The re-run writer (2nd wa-model writer in wt-a) carries the findings.
   427	   327	    const writers = childWithModel(io.__runner, 'wa-model').filter((c) => valAfter(argsOf(c), '-s') === 'workspace-write');
   428	   328	    assert.ok(writers.length >= 2, 'the writer seat re-ran after the BLOCKED review');
   429	   329	    assert.match(argsOf(writers[1]).join(' '), /REVIEW-FINDING-X/, "the re-run writer's prompt carries the review findings");
   430	   330	  });

   432	   332	  it('review-gate cap exhaustion -> escalated, ESCALATION.md, exit 3, no 6th spawn', async () => {
   433	   333	    const io = makePipeRepo({ runner: undefined });
   434	   334	    // Reviewer BLOCKS five times; the 6th writer/reviewer pair must never spawn.
   435	   335	    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [
   436	   336	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'BLOCKED', findings: 'f1' },
   437	   337	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'BLOCKED', findings: 'f2' },
   438	   338	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'BLOCKED', findings: 'f3' },
   439	   339	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'BLOCKED', findings: 'f4' },
   440	   340	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'BLOCKED', findings: 'f5' },
   441	   341	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'BLOCKED', findings: 'f6' }, // must NEVER be consumed
   442	   342	    ]);
   443	   343	    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
   444	   344	    const code = await run(['pipeline', 'run'], io);
   445	   345	    assert.equal(code, 3, 'a per-subtask review gate at the cap escalates');
   446	   346	    assert.ok(loopFile(io, 'ESCALATION.md'), 'an ESCALATION.md is written');
   447	   347	    assert.equal(childWithModel(io.__runner, 'mg-model').length, 0, 'the merger never runs for an escalated subtask');
   448	   348	    // At most five review iterations were attempted (a 6th BLOCKED is never reached).
   449	   349	    const reviews = io.__runner.calls.filter((c) => valAfter(argsOf(c), '-s') === 'read-only');
   450	   350	    assert.ok(reviews.length <= 5, `at most five reviews before escalation; got ${reviews.length}`);
   451	   351	  });
   452	   352	
   453	   353	  it('merger BLOCKED -> the writer retries (same gate-cap discipline)', async () => {

   456	   356	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, // writer 1

   459	   359	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, // writer re-run
   460	   360	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, // reviewer
   461	   361	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, // merger approves
   462	   362	      ...cleanSubtask(),
   463	   363	    ]);
   464	   364	    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
   465	   365	    const code = await run(['pipeline', 'run'], io);
   466	   366	    assert.equal(code, 0, 'the subtask recovers after a merger rejection + writer retry');
   467	   367	    assert.ok(childWithModel(io.__runner, 'wa-model').filter((c) => valAfter(argsOf(c), '-s') === 'workspace-write').length >= 2, 'the writer retried after the merger BLOCKED');
   468	   368	  });
   469	   369	});

   500	   400	describe('pipeline — completion', () => {

   516	   416	function loopFile(io, name) {
   517	   417	  return io.files()[`${loopPaths('/repo').dir}/${name}`];
   518	   418	}

   538	    14	
   539	    15	// Every review seat is read-only — writers are the implementer-side roles only.
   540	    16	const REVIEWER_ROLES = new Set(['plan-reviewer', 'test-verifier', 'subtask-reviewer', 'final-reviewer-a', 'final-reviewer-b']);
   541	    17	
   542	    18	// AGENTS.md §9: children commit solely as the repo owner, zero AI attribution.
   543	    19	const GIT_IDENTITY = Object.freeze({
   544	    20	  GIT_AUTHOR_NAME: 'SakshamUboweja',

   550	    26	/**
   551	    27	 * Assemble the spawn contract for a role child. Reviewers are read-only on
   552	    28	 * every platform; every child is marked supervised (its own baton hooks
   553	    29	 * no-op), carries the sole-author git identity, and has stdin CLOSED at
   554	    30	 * spawn (the codex-exec stdin-hang field failure).
   555	    31	 * @param {{platform: string, role: string, model: string, effort?: string | null, mode?: string}} assignment
   556	    32	 * @param {string} prompt
   557	    33	 * @param {{root: string}} opts
   558	    34	 * @returns {{command: string, args: string[], env: Record<string, string>, stdio: any[]}}
   559	    35	 */
   560	    36	export function buildChildArgv(assignment, prompt, opts) {
   561	    37	  const readOnly = REVIEWER_ROLES.has(assignment.role);
   562	    38	  const env = { BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD: '1', ...GIT_IDENTITY };
   563	    39	  const stdio = ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'];
   564	    40	
   565	    41	  if (assignment.platform === 'claude-code') {
   566	    42	    const args = ['-p', prompt];
   567	    43	    if (readOnly) {
   568	    44	      args.push('--permission-mode', 'default', '--allowedTools', 'Read,Grep,Glob');
   569	    45	    } else {
   570	    46	      args.push('--permission-mode', 'acceptEdits', '--allowedTools', 'Read,Grep,Glob,Write,Edit,Bash');
   571	    47	    }
   572	    48	    return { command: 'claude', args, env, stdio };
   573	    49	  }
   574	    50	
   575	    51	  // codex (and the default shape for exec-style CLIs).
   576	    52	  const args = ['exec', '-C', opts.root, '-s', readOnly ? 'read-only' : 'workspace-write', '--model', assignment.model];
   577	    53	  if (assignment.effort) args.push('-c', `model_reasoning_effort=${assignment.effort}`);
   578	    54	  args.push(prompt);
   579	    55	  return { command: 'codex', args, env, stdio };
   580	    56	}
   581	    57	
   582	    58	const VERDICT_RE = /VERDICT:\s*(APPROVED_WITH_NOTES|APPROVED|BLOCKED)\b/g;
   583	    59	
   584	    60	/** @param {string} region @returns {{verdict: string, findings: string} | null} */
   585	    61	function lastVerdictIn(region) {
   586	    62	  let last = null;
   587	    63	  for (const m of region.matchAll(VERDICT_RE)) last = m;
   588	    64	  if (!last) return null;
   589	    65	  const after = region.slice((last.index ?? 0) + last[0].length);
   590	    66	  return { verdict: last[1], findings: after.trim() };
   591	    67	}
   592	    68	
   593	    69	/**
   594	    70	 * Region-bounded verdict parsing — the prompt region (which quotes the
   595	    71	 * verdict format in its instructions) is NEVER scanned, and there is no
   596	    72	 * whole-transcript fallback. Unparseable is BLOCKED, never APPROVED.
   597	    73	 * - codex: only the text after the LAST line starting with 'tokens used'.
   598	    74	 * - claude-code: only the `result` field of the structured JSON output.
   599	    75	 * Multiple tails inside the trusted region → the LAST one wins.
   600	    76	 * @param {string} transcript @param {{platform: string}} opts
   601	    77	 * @returns {{verdict: string, findings: string, reason?: string}}
   602	    78	 */
   603	    79	export function parseVerdict(transcript, { platform }) {
   604	    80	  const unparseable = { verdict: 'BLOCKED', findings: '', reason: 'unparseable' };
   605	    81	  const text = String(transcript ?? '');
   606	    82	
   607	    83	  if (platform === 'claude-code') {
   608	    84	    let obj = null;
   609	    85	    try {
   610	    86	      obj = JSON.parse(text.trim());
   611	    87	    } catch {
   612	    88	      return unparseable;
   613	    89	    }
   614	    90	    if (typeof obj?.result !== 'string') return unparseable;
   615	    91	    const hit = lastVerdictIn(obj.result);
   616	    92	    return hit ?? unparseable;
   617	    93	  }
   618	    94	
   619	    95	  // codex: locate the LAST 'tokens used' marker line; the trusted region is
   620	    96	  // strictly after it.
   621	    97	  const lines = text.split('\n');
   622	    98	  let markerLine = -1;
   623	    99	  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i += 1) {
   624	   100	    if (/^tokens used\b/i.test(lines[i])) markerLine = i;
   625	   101	  }
   626	   102	  if (markerLine === -1) return unparseable;
   627	   103	  const region = lines.slice(markerLine + 1).join('\n');
   628	   104	  const hit = lastVerdictIn(region);
   629	   105	  return hit ?? unparseable;
   630	   106	}
   631	   107	
   632	   108	const TRUNCATION_MARKER = '\n[... truncated by baton log cap ...]\n';
   633	   109	
   634	   110	/**
   635	   111	 * Cap a transcript: head preserved, middle replaced with a marker, TAIL
   636	   112	 * preserved verbatim (the verdict lives at the end and must never be cut).
   637	   113	 * @param {string} text @param {number} maxBytes
   638	   114	 * @returns {string}
   639	   115	 */
   640	   116	export function capLog(text, maxBytes) {
   641	   117	  const s = String(text ?? '');
   642	   118	  if (Buffer.byteLength(s) <= maxBytes) return s;
   643	   119	  const headBytes = Math.max(1, Math.floor(maxBytes * 0.4));
   644	   120	  const tailBytes = Math.max(1, maxBytes - headBytes);
   645	   121	  const buf = Buffer.from(s);
   646	   122	  const head = buf.subarray(0, headBytes).toString();
   647	   123	  const tail = buf.subarray(buf.length - tailBytes).toString();
   648	   124	  return head + TRUNCATION_MARKER + tail;
   649	   125	}
   650	   126	
   651	   127	/**
   652	   128	 * Exit classification delegates to core detect — one classifier, one
   653	   129	 * signature table, no drift. Returns exactly classify(...).class.
   654	   130	 * @param {string} transcript @param {number} exitCode @param {string} platform @param {any} table
   655	   131	 * @returns {string}
   656	   132	 */
   657	   133	export function classifyChildExit(transcript, exitCode, platform, table) {
   658	   134	  return classify({ text: transcript, exitCode, platform, table }).class;
   659	   135	}
   660	   136	
   661	   137	/** @param {number} pid @param {NodeJS.Signals} sig */
   662	   138	function killGroup(pid, sig) {
   663	   139	  try {
   664	   140	    process.kill(-pid, sig);
   665	   141	  } catch {
   666	   142	    // ESRCH — already gone.
   667	   143	  }
   668	   144	}
   669	   145	
   670	   146	/**
   671	   147	 * Spawn and supervise one child: detached in its OWN process group, stdin
   672	   148	 * closed; on timeout SIGTERM the group, wait graceMs, SIGKILL the group (no
   673	   149	 * zombie grandchildren). Output streams to a capped log at opts.logPath; the
   674	   150	 * verdict is parsed from the full transcript, region-bounded.
   675	   151	 * @param {{command: string, args: string[], env?: Record<string, string>, cwd?: string}} spec
   676	   152	 * @param {{timeoutMs: number, graceMs: number, logPath: string, maxLogBytes?: number, platform?: string}} opts
   677	   153	 * @returns {Promise<{timedOut: boolean, exitCode: number | null, verdict?: string, findings?: string, reason?: string, logPath: string}>}
   678	   154	 */
   679	   155	export function superviseChild(spec, opts) {
   680	   156	  return new Promise((resolve) => {
   681	   157	    const maxLogBytes = opts.maxLogBytes ?? 1_000_000;
   682	   158	    const child = spawn(spec.command, spec.args, {
   683	   159	      cwd: spec.cwd,
   684	   160	      env: { ...process.env, ...(spec.env ?? {}) },
   685	   161	      stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
   686	   162	      detached: true, // its own process group — the kill unit
   687	   163	    });
   688	   164	
   689	   165	    let out = '';
   690	   166	    let timedOut = false;
   691	   167	    /** @type {NodeJS.Timeout | null} */
   692	   168	    let killTimer = null;
   693	   169	
   694	   170	    const onChunk = (/** @type {Buffer} */ c) => {
   695	   171	      out += c.toString();
   696	   172	    };
   697	   173	    child.stdout?.on('data', onChunk);
   698	   174	    child.stderr?.on('data', onChunk);
   699	   175	
   700	   176	    const timeout = setTimeout(() => {
   701	   177	      timedOut = true;
   702	   178	      if (typeof child.pid === 'number') {
   703	   179	        killGroup(child.pid, 'SIGTERM');
   704	   180	        killTimer = setTimeout(() => {
   705	   181	          if (typeof child.pid === 'number') killGroup(child.pid, 'SIGKILL');
   706	   182	        }, opts.graceMs);
   707	   183	      }
   708	   184	    }, opts.timeoutMs);
   709	   185	
   710	   186	    const finish = (/** @type {number | null} */ exitCode) => {
   711	   187	      clearTimeout(timeout);
   712	   188	      if (killTimer) clearTimeout(killTimer);
   713	   189	      // Belt-and-braces: reap any group survivors even on a clean exit path.
   714	   190	      if (timedOut && typeof child.pid === 'number') killGroup(child.pid, 'SIGKILL');
   715	   191	      try {
   716	   192	        mkdirSync(dirname(opts.logPath), { recursive: true });
   717	   193	        writeFileSync(opts.logPath, capLog(out, maxLogBytes));
   718	   194	      } catch {
   719	   195	        // The log must never mask the result.
   720	   196	      }
   721	   197	      const parsed = parseVerdict(out, { platform: opts.platform ?? 'codex' });
   722	   198	      resolve({
   723	   199	        timedOut,
   724	   200	        exitCode: timedOut ? null : exitCode,
   725	   201	        verdict: parsed.verdict,
   726	   202	        findings: parsed.findings,
   727	   203	        ...(parsed.reason ? { reason: parsed.reason } : {}),
   728	   204	        logPath: opts.logPath,
   729	   205	      });
   730	   206	    };
   731	   207	
   732	   208	    child.on('error', () => finish(null));
   733	   209	    child.on('close', (code) => finish(code));
   734	   210	  });
   735	   211	}

   744	     5	/** Audit note for a refused payload — visible in the decision log, never a throw. */
   745	     6	const refusalNote = (/** @type {string} */ type, /** @type {any} */ detail) =>
   746	     7	  `[${type} with invalid payload refused: ${JSON.stringify(detail).slice(0, 120)}]`;
   747	     8	
   748	     9	/**
   749	    10	 * Pure event reducer. Returns a new bundle; never mutates the input.
   750	    11	 * Idempotent: an event whose dedupeKey is still in the bundle's ring is a no-op.
   751	    12	 * Unknown event types degrade to a decision-log note — a checkpoint must never
   752	    13	 * fail because a newer adapter emitted something this core doesn't know — and
   753	    14	 * journal payloads are untrusted (gate-2 fix 6): malformed shapes degrade to a
   754	    15	 * refusal note and repository paths are jailed before storage.
   755	    16	 * @param {any} bundle
   756	    17	 * @param {{seq: number, ts: string, type: string, dedupeKey: string, writerId?: string, source?: string, payload: any}} event
   757	    18	 */
   758	    19	export function applyEvent(bundle, event) {
   759	    20	  // Journal lines are untrusted (gate-2 iter-2 B4): a parseable null/number/
   760	    21	  // string is a valid JSON line but not an event envelope — degrade to a
   761	    22	  // no-op rather than crash replay. The store's load loop also pre-filters and
   762	    23	  // warns; this is the last-line guard for any direct caller.
   763	    24	  if (event === null || typeof event !== 'object' || Array.isArray(event)) return bundle;
   764	    25	  if (bundle.dedupeRing.includes(event.dedupeKey)) return bundle;
   765	    26	
   766	    27	  const out = structuredClone(bundle);
   767	    28	  const { seq, ts, type, payload = {} } = structuredClone(event);
   768	    29	
   769	    30	  switch (type) {
   770	    31	    case 'decision': {
   771	    32	      /** @type {{seq: number, ts: string, summary: any, detail?: any}} */
   772	    33	      const entry = { seq, ts, summary: payload.summary };
   773	    34	      if (payload.detail !== undefined) entry.detail = payload.detail;
   774	    35	      out.decisions.push(entry);
   775	    36	      break;
   776	    37	    }
   777	    38	    case 'note':
   778	    39	      out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: payload.text });
   779	    40	      // A structured StopFailure rate_limit note marks the open bundle
   780	    41	      // limit-hit (gate-2 fix 3): the unsealed limit death must be visible to
   781	    42	      // the next SessionStart on any platform, not buried in the decision log.
   782	    43	      if (payload.structured?.kind === 'stop-failure' && payload.errorType === 'rate_limit' && out.handoff.status === 'open') {
   783	    44	        out.handoff = { ...out.handoff, reason: out.handoff.reason ?? payload.text ?? null, reasonClass: 'usage-limit' };
   784	    45	      }
   785	    46	      break;
   786	    47	    case 'plan.set':
   787	    48	      if (Array.isArray(payload.steps) && payload.steps.every((/** @type {any} */ s) => s !== null && typeof s === 'object')) {
   788	    49	        out.plan.steps = payload.steps;
   789	    50	      } else {
   790	    51	        out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: refusalNote('plan.set', payload?.steps) });
   791	    52	      }
   792	    53	      break;
   793	    54	    case 'plan.step': {
   794	    55	      if (payload === null || typeof payload !== 'object' || typeof payload.id !== 'string') {
   795	    56	        out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: refusalNote('plan.step', payload) });
   796	    57	        break;
   797	    58	      }
   798	    59	      const existing = out.plan.steps.find((/** @type {any} */ s) => s.id === payload.id);
   799	    60	      if (existing) Object.assign(existing, payload);
   800	    61	      else out.plan.steps.push(payload);
   801	    62	      break;
   802	    63	    }
   803	    64	    case 'file.touch': {
   804	    65	      const safePath = jailRelPath(payload?.path);
   805	    66	      if (safePath === null) {
   806	    67	        out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: refusalNote('file.touch', payload?.path) });
   807	    68	        break;
   808	    69	      }
   809	    70	      const entry = { path: safePath, op: typeof payload.op === 'string' ? payload.op : 'touch', lastTs: ts };
   810	    71	      const i = out.files.touched.findIndex((/** @type {any} */ f) => f.path === safePath);
   811	    72	      if (i === -1) out.files.touched.push(entry);
   812	    73	      else out.files.touched[i] = entry;
   813	    74	      break;
   814	    75	    }
   815	    76	    case 'roles.remap': {
   816	    77	      // The assignments object AND every value must be non-null objects (iter-3
   817	    78	      // F3): a well-formed {planner: null} used to be stored verbatim and then
   818	    79	      // crashed renderHandoffMd. Untrusted journal input degrades to a note.
   819	    80	      const a = payload?.assignments;
   820	    81	      const wellFormed =
   821	    82	        a !== null &&
   822	    83	        typeof a === 'object' &&
   823	    84	        !Array.isArray(a) &&
   824	    85	        Object.values(a).every((/** @type {any} */ v) => v !== null && typeof v === 'object' && !Array.isArray(v));
   825	    86	      if (wellFormed) {
   826	    87	        out.roles.assignments = a;
   827	    88	      } else {
   828	    89	        out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: refusalNote('roles.remap', a) });
   829	    90	      }
   830	    91	      break;
   831	    92	    }
   832	    93	    case 'task.update': {
   833	    94	      /** @type {Record<string, any>} */
   834	    95	      const patch = {};
   835	    96	      if (typeof payload?.goal === 'string') patch.goal = payload.goal;
   836	    97	      if (Array.isArray(payload?.constraints) && payload.constraints.every((/** @type {any} */ c) => typeof c === 'string')) {
   837	    98	        patch.constraints = payload.constraints;
   838	    99	      }
   839	   100	      if (Array.isArray(payload?.acceptance)) patch.acceptance = payload.acceptance;
   840	   101	      if (Object.keys(patch).length === 0) {
   841	   102	        out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: refusalNote('task.update', payload) });
   842	   103	        break;
   843	   104	      }
   844	   105	      out.task = { ...out.task, ...patch };
   845	   106	      break;
   846	   107	    }
   847	   108	    case 'git.update':
   848	   109	      if (payload === null || typeof payload === 'object') out.git = payload;
   849	   110	      else out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: refusalNote('git.update', payload) });
   850	   111	      break;
   851	   112	    case 'bundle.seed':
   852	   113	      // Self-applying identity (gate-2 iter-2 B6): the auto-seed emits this as
   853	   114	      // the journal's first event so a journal-only rebuild (snapshot + .bak
   854	   115	      // both gone) restores the bundle's identity/origin/task instead of the
   855	   116	      // "unknown" placeholder resolveBase seeds. Replayed first (lowest seq),
   856	   117	      // so later events layer on top; on normal replay its seq is below the
   857	   118	      // snapshot's journalSeq and it is skipped.
   858	   119	      if (payload && typeof payload === 'object') {
   859	   120	        if (typeof payload.bundleId === 'string') out.bundleId = payload.bundleId;
   860	   121	        if (typeof payload.generation === 'number') out.generation = payload.generation;
   861	   122	        if (typeof payload.createdAt === 'string') out.createdAt = payload.createdAt;
   862	   123	        // Restore identity ONLY (platform/model) — never sessionHint/unstable.
   863	   124	        // Session ownership is adopted AFTER the seed (a snapshot-only mutation),
   864	   125	        // so replaying the seed must not clobber a later-adopted hint back to
   865	   126	        // its seed-time null (gate-2 iter-2: caught by the adoption test).
   866	   127	        if (payload.origin && typeof payload.origin === 'object') {
   867	   128	          if (typeof payload.origin.platform === 'string') out.origin.platform = payload.origin.platform;
   868	   129	          if (typeof payload.origin.model === 'string') out.origin.model = payload.origin.model;
   869	   130	        }
   870	   131	        if (payload.task && typeof payload.task === 'object' && typeof payload.task.goal === 'string') {
   871	   132	          out.task.goal = payload.task.goal;
   872	   133	        }
   873	   134	      }
   874	   135	      break;
   875	   136	    case 'transcript.set':
   876	   137	      // Opt-in transcript tail (plan §Transcript policy): stored under the
   877	   138	      // literal property name `transcript` so purge-transcript strips it from
   878	   139	      // every retained copy. Render never includes it.
   879	   140	      if (payload?.transcript !== null && typeof payload?.transcript === 'object' && typeof payload.transcript.tail === 'string') {
   880	   141	        out.transcript = {
   881	   142	          capturedAt: typeof payload.transcript.capturedAt === 'string' ? payload.transcript.capturedAt : ts,
   882	   143	          tail: payload.transcript.tail,
   883	   144	        };
   884	   145	      } else {
   885	   146	        out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: refusalNote('transcript.set', 'malformed payload') });
   886	   147	      }
   887	   148	      break;
   888	   149	    default:
   889	   150	      out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: `[unhandled event type '${type}' recorded as note]` });
   890	   151	      break;
   891	   152	  }
   892	   153	
   893	   154	  out.updatedAt = ts;
   894	   155	  // Harden against foreign/keyless entries (gate-2 fix): a missing seq must
   895	   156	  // never poison journalSeq to NaN, and an absent dedupeKey must never enter
   896	   157	  // the ring (undefined in the ring dedupes every later keyless event).
   897	   158	  if (typeof seq === 'number' && Number.isFinite(seq)) out.journalSeq = Math.max(out.journalSeq, seq);
   898	   159	  if (typeof event.dedupeKey === 'string' && event.dedupeKey.length > 0) {
   899	   160	    out.dedupeRing.push(event.dedupeKey);
   900	   161	    if (out.dedupeRing.length > RING_CAP) out.dedupeRing.splice(0, out.dedupeRing.length - RING_CAP);
   901	   162	  }
   902	   163	
   903	   164	  return out;
   904	   165	}

   938	    30	/** @param {string} root */
   939	    31	export function worktreePaths(root) {
   940	    32	  const dir = `${root}/.worktrees`;
   941	    33	  /** @type {Record<string, string>} */
   942	    34	  const seats = { a: `${dir}/wt-a`, b: `${dir}/wt-b` };
   943	    35	  return { dir, seats };
   944	    36	}
   945	    37	
   946	    38	/** @param {string} seat */
   947	    39	const seatBranch = (seat) => `baton/wt-${seat}/base`;
   948	    40	
   949	    41	/** @param {any} io @param {string} cwd @param {string[]} args */
   950	    42	async function git(io, cwd, args) {
   951	    43	  return io.execFile('git', args, { cwd });
   952	    44	}
   953	    45	
   954	    46	/** Parse `git worktree list --porcelain` into [{path, branch}]. @param {string} out */
   955	    47	function parseWorktreeList(out) {
   956	    48	  /** @type {Array<{path: string, branch: string | null}>} */
   957	    49	  const entries = [];
   958	    50	  for (const block of String(out).split(/\n\n+/)) {
   959	    51	    const path = block.match(/^worktree (.+)$/m)?.[1];
   960	    52	    if (!path) continue;
   961	    53	    const branch = block.match(/^branch refs\/heads\/(.+)$/m)?.[1] ?? null;
   962	    54	    entries.push({ path, branch });
   963	    55	  }
   964	    56	  return entries;
   965	    57	}
   966	    58	
   967	    59	/**
   968	    60	 * Ensure both seat worktrees exist on their namespaced branches and that
   969	    61	 * `.worktrees/` is gitignored. Idempotent: an existing seat is left alone.
   970	    62	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io
   971	    63	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: boolean, worktrees: Array<{seat: string, path: string, branch: string}>, gitignoreChanged: boolean}>}
   972	    64	 */
   973	    65	export async function setupWorktrees(root, io) {
   974	    66	  const p = worktreePaths(root);
   975	    67	
   976	    68	  const gitignorePath = `${root}/.gitignore`;
   977	    69	  const existing = io.fs.existsSync(gitignorePath) ? io.fs.readFileSync(gitignorePath, 'utf8') : '';
   978	    70	  const ensured = ensureIgnoreLine(existing, '.worktrees/');
   979	    71	  if (ensured.changed) io.fs.writeFileSync(gitignorePath, ensured.text);
   980	    72	
   981	    73	  const listed = parseWorktreeList((await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'list', '--porcelain'])).stdout);
   982	    74	  /** @type {Array<{seat: string, path: string, branch: string}>} */
   983	    75	  const worktrees = [];
   984	    76	  for (const seat of SEATS) {
   985	    77	    const path = p.seats[seat];
   986	    78	    const present = listed.find((e) => e.path === path);
   987	    79	    if (present) {
   988	    80	      worktrees.push({ seat, path, branch: present.branch ?? seatBranch(seat) });
   989	    81	      continue;
   990	    82	    }
   991	    83	    const branch = seatBranch(seat);
   992	    84	    await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'add', '-b', branch, path]);
   993	    85	    worktrees.push({ seat, path, branch });
   994	    86	  }
   995	    87	  return { ok: true, worktrees, gitignoreChanged: ensured.changed };
   996	    88	}
   997	    89	
   998	    90	/**
   999	    91	 * Preflight before a child runs in a seat: the worktree is listed, its index
  1000	    92	 * is clean, and HEAD sits on the expected branch. Refusals name the failed
  1001	    93	 * check and never issue destructive git.
  1002	    94	 * @param {string} root @param {{seat: string, branch: string}} target @param {any} io
  1003	    95	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true} | {ok: false, check: string, refusal: string}>}
  1004	    96	 */
  1005	    97	export async function preflightWorktree(root, target, io) {
  1006	    98	  const path = worktreePaths(root).seats[target.seat];
  1007	    99	  const listed = parseWorktreeList((await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'list', '--porcelain'])).stdout);
  1008	   100	  if (!listed.some((e) => e.path === path)) {
  1009	   101	    return { ok: false, check: 'missing-worktree', refusal: `worktree ${path} is not listed by git — run setup or self-heal first` };
  1010	   102	  }
  1011	   103	  const status = (await git(io, path, ['status', '--porcelain'])).stdout.trim();
  1012	   104	  if (status.length > 0) {
  1013	   105	    return { ok: false, check: 'dirty-index', refusal: `worktree ${path} has a dirty index/working tree — a clean seat is required before a child runs` };
  1014	   106	  }
  1015	   107	  const head = (await git(io, path, ['rev-parse', '--abbrev-ref', 'HEAD'])).stdout.trim();
  1016	   108	  if (head !== target.branch) {
  1017	   109	    return { ok: false, check: 'wrong-branch', refusal: `worktree ${path} HEAD is on '${head}', expected branch '${target.branch}'` };
  1018	   110	  }
  1019	   111	  return { ok: true };
  1020	   112	}
  1021	   113	
  1022	   114	/**
  1023	   115	 * Postflight after a child: HEAD still on the expected branch (checked in the
  1024	   116	 * seat cwd) and main unmoved from the pre-child capture.
  1025	   117	 * @param {string} root @param {{seat: string, branch: string, mainSha: string}} target @param {any} io
  1026	   118	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true} | {ok: false, check: string, refusal: string}>}
  1027	   119	 */
  1028	   120	export async function postflightWorktree(root, target, io) {
  1029	   121	  const path = worktreePaths(root).seats[target.seat];
  1030	   122	  const head = (await git(io, path, ['rev-parse', '--abbrev-ref', 'HEAD'])).stdout.trim();
  1031	   123	  if (head !== target.branch) {
  1032	   124	    return { ok: false, check: 'head-drift', refusal: `the child left HEAD on '${head}' instead of the expected branch '${target.branch}'` };
  1033	   125	  }
  1034	   126	  const main = (await git(io, root, ['rev-parse', 'main'])).stdout.trim();
  1035	   127	  if (main !== target.mainSha) {
  1036	   128	    return { ok: false, check: 'main-moved', refusal: `main moved under the child (was ${target.mainSha}, now ${main}) — only the merger writes main` };
  1037	   129	  }
  1038	   130	  return { ok: true };
  1039	   131	}
  1040	   132	
  1041	   133	/**
  1042	   134	 * Full-range attribution scan over main..branch — the merge gate. Every
  1043	   135	 * commit must be authored AND committed by the sole author, with no forbidden
  1044	   136	 * AI-attribution trailer in the body. The range is never windowed.
  1045	   137	 * @param {string} root @param {string} branch @param {any} io
  1046	   138	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true} | {ok: false, offending: string, reason: string}>}
  1047	   139	 */
  1048	   140	export async function attributionScan(root, branch, io) {
  1049	   141	  const out = (await git(io, root, ['log', `main..${branch}`, `--format=${LOG_FORMAT}`])).stdout;
  1050	   142	  for (const record of String(out).split('\x1e')) {
  1051	   143	    if (record.trim().length === 0) continue;
  1052	   144	    const [sha, an, ae, cn, ce, body = ''] = record.replace(/^\n/, '').split('\x00');
  1053	   145	    if (an !== SOLE_AUTHOR.name || ae !== SOLE_AUTHOR.email) {
  1054	   146	      return { ok: false, offending: sha, reason: `commit ${sha} has a foreign author identity (${an} <${ae}>) — sole-author is required` };
  1055	   147	    }
  1056	   148	    if (cn !== SOLE_AUTHOR.name || ce !== SOLE_AUTHOR.email) {
  1057	   149	      return { ok: false, offending: sha, reason: `commit ${sha} has a foreign committer identity (${cn} <${ce}>) — sole-author is required` };
  1058	   150	    }
  1059	   151	    if (TRAILER_PATTERNS.some((re) => re.test(body))) {
  1060	   152	      return { ok: false, offending: sha, reason: `commit ${sha} carries a forbidden AI-attribution trailer (co-authored/generated-with/emoji) in its message` };
  1061	   153	    }
  1062	   154	  }
  1063	   155	  return { ok: true };
  1064	   156	}
  1065	   157	
  1066	   158	const mergeLockPath = (/** @type {string} */ root) => `${root}/.handoff/loop/merge.lock`;
  1067	   159	
  1068	   160	/**
  1069	   161	 * Merge an approved subtask branch into main — one guarded transaction:
  1070	   162	 * acquire the merge lock (a held lock refuses), gate on the attribution scan,
  1071	   163	 * merge at the repo root, abort-and-park on conflict, then ff-only sync both
  1072	   164	 * seats. The lock is released on EVERY exit path after acquisition.
  1073	   165	 * @param {string} root @param {{branch: string}} input @param {any} io
  1074	   166	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true, branch: string} | {ok: false, reason: string}>}
  1075	   167	 */
  1076	   168	export async function mergeSubtask(root, input, io) {
  1077	   169	  const lock = mergeLockPath(root);
  1078	   170	  if (io.fs.existsSync(lock)) {
  1079	   171	    return { ok: false, reason: `the merge lock at ${lock} is held by another merger — refusing a concurrent merge` };
  1080	   172	  }
  1081	   173	  ensureDir(io.fs, `${root}/.handoff/loop`);
  1082	   174	  atomicWriteJson(io.fs, lock, { host: io.host, pid: io.pid, at: io.now() });
  1083	   175	  try {
  1084	   176	    const scan = await attributionScan(root, input.branch, io);
  1085	   177	    if (scan.ok !== true) return { ok: false, reason: scan.reason };
  1086	   178	
  1087	   179	    try {
  1088	   180	      await git(io, root, ['merge', input.branch]);
  1089	   181	    } catch (err) {
  1090	   182	      // Conflict (or any merge failure): abort, never auto-resolve — park.
  1091	   183	      try {
  1092	   184	        await git(io, root, ['merge', '--abort']);
  1093	   185	      } catch {
  1094	   186	        // Nothing to abort — the failure predates a merge state.
  1095	   187	      }
  1096	   188	      const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.stdout || /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message || String(err);
  1097	   189	      return { ok: false, reason: `merge conflict — aborted and parked for the operator: ${String(msg).split('\n')[0]}` };
  1098	   190	    }
  1099	   191	
  1100	   192	    const seats = worktreePaths(root).seats;
  1101	   193	    for (const seat of SEATS) {
  1102	   194	      try {
  1103	   195	        await git(io, seats[seat], ['merge', '--ff-only', 'main']);
  1104	   196	      } catch (err) {
  1105	   197	        const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.stderr || /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message || String(err);
  1106	   198	        return { ok: false, reason: `worktree wt-${seat} could not fast-forward to main — corruption signal, parked: ${String(msg).split('\n')[0]}` };
  1107	   199	      }
  1108	   200	    }
  1109	   201	    return { ok: true, branch: input.branch };
  1110	   202	  } finally {
  1111	   203	    try {
  1112	   204	      if (io.fs.existsSync(lock)) io.fs.unlinkSync(lock);
  1113	   205	    } catch {
  1114	   206	      // Best effort — a leaked lock is visible and operator-recoverable.
  1115	   207	    }
  1116	   208	  }
  1117	   209	}
  1118	   210	
  1119	   211	/**
  1120	   212	 * Self-heal a raw-deleted seat: git still lists it but the directory is gone
  1121	   213	 * — prune the stale metadata and re-add on the same branch.
  1122	   214	 * @param {string} root @param {{seat: string, branch: string}} target @param {any} io
  1123	   215	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: boolean, healed: boolean}>}
  1124	   216	 */
  1125	   217	export async function selfHealWorktree(root, target, io) {
  1126	   218	  const path = worktreePaths(root).seats[target.seat];
  1127	   219	  const listed = parseWorktreeList((await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'list', '--porcelain'])).stdout);
  1128	   220	  const stale = listed.some((e) => e.path === path) && !io.fs.existsSync(path);
  1129	   221	  if (!stale) return { ok: true, healed: false };
  1130	   222	  await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'prune']);
  1131	   223	  await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'add', path, target.branch]);
  1132	   224	  return { ok: true, healed: true };
  1133	   225	}
  1134	   226	
  1135	   227	/**
  1136	   228	 * Tear down BOTH seats: remove the worktrees and delete their namespaced
  1137	   229	 * branches. Anything outside .worktrees/wt-* or baton/wt-* is never touched,
  1138	   230	 * so a teardown leaves the repo a plain git repo (acceptance constraint 5).
  1139	   231	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io
  1140	   232	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: boolean, removed: string[]}>}
  1141	   233	 */
  1142	   234	export async function teardownWorktrees(root, io) {
  1143	   235	  const p = worktreePaths(root);
  1144	   236	  const listed = parseWorktreeList((await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'list', '--porcelain'])).stdout);
  1145	   237	  /** @type {string[]} */
  1146	   238	  const removed = [];
  1147	   239	  for (const seat of SEATS) {
  1148	   240	    const entry = listed.find((e) => e.path === p.seats[seat]);
  1149	   241	    if (!entry) continue;
  1150	   242	    await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'remove', '--force', p.seats[seat]]);
  1151	   243	    if (entry.branch && WORKTREE_BRANCH_RE.test(entry.branch)) {
  1152	   244	      await git(io, root, ['branch', '-D', entry.branch]);
  1153	   245	    }
  1154	   246	    removed.push(seat);
  1155	   247	  }
  1156	   248	  return { ok: true, removed };
  1157	   249	}
  1158	   250	
  1159	   251	/**
  1160	   252	 * Branch jail: only baton/wt-* branches may ever be deleted through this
  1161	   253	 * layer. An out-of-namespace ref refuses without touching git.
  1162	   254	 * @param {string} root @param {string} branch @param {any} io
  1163	   255	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true} | {ok: false, error: string}>}
  1164	   256	 */
  1165	   257	export async function deleteBranch(root, branch, io) {
  1166	   258	  if (!WORKTREE_BRANCH_RE.test(branch)) {
  1167	   259	    return { ok: false, error: `refused: '${branch}' is outside the baton/wt- namespace — the loop never deletes non-loop branches` };
  1168	   260	  }
  1169	   261	  await git(io, root, ['branch', '-D', branch]);
  1170	   262	  return { ok: true };
  1171	   263	}
