     1	import { normSep, joinNorm } from '../util/pathnorm.mjs';
     2	
     3	/**
     4	 * The --json contract: exactly one compact envelope line on stdout, nothing else.
     5	 * @param {any} io
     6	 * @param {{ok: boolean, data?: any, warnings?: any[], error?: {code: string, msg: string} | null}} env
     7	 */
     8	export function emitEnvelope(io, env) {
     9	  const full = { ok: env.ok, data: env.data ?? null, warnings: env.warnings ?? [], error: env.error ?? null };
    10	  io.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(full) + '\n');
    11	}
    12	
    13	/**
    14	 * Centralized usage-error emission (gate-2 fix 11): with --json the envelope
    15	 * contract holds even for bad invocations; otherwise a one-line stderr
    16	 * message. Always returns the usage exit code 2.
    17	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
    18	 * @param {string} cmd @param {string} msg
    19	 * @returns {number}
    20	 */
    21	export function usageError(io, flags, cmd, msg) {
    22	  if (flags.json === true) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg } });
    23	  else io.stderr.write(`baton ${cmd}: ${msg}\n`);
    24	  return 2;
    25	}
    26	
    27	/**
    28	 * Repo-root discovery (plan §Root discovery): `--root` wins; else the nearest
    29	 * ancestor of io.cwd carrying `.handoff/` or `baton.config.json`; else the
    30	 * nearest ancestor carrying `.git` (the repo toplevel — and a boundary the
    31	 * walk never crosses, so a nested repo resolves to itself); else io.cwd.
    32	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
    33	 * @returns {string}
    34	 */
    35	export function resolveRoot(io, flags) {
    36	  if (typeof flags.root === 'string' && flags.root.length > 0) return flags.root;
    37	  // Normalize separators up front (gate-2 iter-2 M2): a Windows cwd arrives
    38	  // backslash-separated, but Node fs accepts forward slashes, so the ascent
    39	  // and the marker checks work in one forward-slash space. The last separator
    40	  // is either kind; the loop stops at a drive/UNC or filesystem root.
    41	  const start = normSep(io.cwd);
    42	  let dir = start;
    43	  while (true) {
    44	    try {
    45	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.handoff')) || io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, 'baton.config.json'))) return dir;
    46	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.git'))) return dir; // repo toplevel; never walk past a .git boundary
    47	    } catch {
    48	      break;
    49	    }
    50	    const cut = dir.lastIndexOf('/');
    51	    if (cut < 0) break;
    52	    const parent = cut === 0 ? '/' : dir.slice(0, cut);
    53	    // At a drive-relative ceiling ("C:") examine the drive ROOT ("C:/") once —
    54	    // a repo can live directly at C:/ (iter-3 F6) — then stop.
    55	    if (/^[A-Za-z]:$/.test(parent)) {
    56	      const driveRoot = `${parent}/`;
    57	      if (driveRoot !== dir) {
    58	        dir = driveRoot;
    59	        continue;
    60	      }
    61	      break;
    62	    }
    63	    if (parent === dir) break;
    64	    dir = parent;
    65	  }
    66	  return start;
    67	}
    68	
    69	/**
    70	 * True when this process is a loop-supervised child (BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD
    71	 * set to any non-empty value). Supervised children must never read or write
    72	 * any bundle and never receive resume context — hook-invoked commands check
    73	 * this BEFORE flag parsing and silently no-op (exit 0, empty output) so the
    74	 * supervisor never has to filter child output or nonzero exits.
    75	 * @param {any} io
    76	 * @returns {boolean}
    77	 */
    78	export function isSupervisedChild(io) {
    79	  const v = io?.env?.BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD;
    80	  return typeof v === 'string' && v.length > 0;
    81	}
    82	
    83	/** The supported platform enum, validated wherever a platform id is intake. */
    84	export const PLATFORMS = ['claude-code', 'codex', 'cursor'];
    85	
    86	/**
    87	 * Validate a platform id against the enum (surface-audit fold): a typo'd
    88	 * platform used to silently seed persistent state whose bogus origin then
    89	 * rejected the REAL platform's checkpoints as foreign.
    90	 * @param {string | boolean | undefined} value
    91	 * @returns {string | null} an error message, or null when valid/absent
    92	 */
    93	export function platformError(value) {
    94	  if (value === undefined) return null;
    95	  if (typeof value === 'string' && PLATFORMS.includes(value)) return null;
    96	  return `platform must be one of ${PLATFORMS.join('|')} (got ${JSON.stringify(value)})`;
    97	}
    98	
    99	/**
   100	 * Strict flag parser (surface-audit fold; docs/design/core.md §tooling always
   101	 * specified strict parsing). spec maps each flag name to 'string' | 'boolean';
   102	 * `json` and `root` are implied common flags. Errors — unknown flag, stray
   103	 * positional, missing string-flag value — return {error} so the command exits
   104	 * 2 instead of silently losing intent: the lenient parser let a stray token
   105	 * flip SAFETY flags off (checkpoint/doctor --strict, remap --native-only,
   106	 * receive --print-prompt all read fail-dangerous exit 0).
   107	 * @param {string[]} args
   108	 * @param {Record<string, 'string' | 'boolean'>} spec
   109	 * @returns {{flags: Record<string, string | boolean>, error?: string}}
   110	 */
   111	export function parseFlagsStrict(args, spec) {
   112	  /** @type {Record<string, 'string' | 'boolean'>} */
   113	  const full = { json: 'boolean', root: 'string', ...spec };
   114	  /** @type {Record<string, string | boolean>} */
   115	  const flags = {};
   116	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
   117	    const a = args[i];
   118	    if (!a.startsWith('--')) return { flags, error: `unexpected argument '${a}' — flags are --name [value]` };
   119	    const key = a.slice(2);
   120	    const kind = full[key];
   121	    if (kind === undefined) return { flags, error: `unknown flag --${key}` };
   122	    if (kind === 'boolean') {
   123	      // A following token that is not a flag would have been this flag's
   124	      // silent "value" under the lenient parser; reject it loudly.
   125	      const next = args[i + 1];
   126	      if (next !== undefined && !next.startsWith('--')) {
   127	        return { flags, error: `unexpected token '${next}' after --${key} (a bare flag takes no value)` };
   128	      }
   129	      flags[key] = true;
   130	      continue;
   131	    }
   132	    const next = args[i + 1];
   133	    // A string flag consumes the next token unless it is itself a KNOWN flag
   134	    // (so `--commit --origin x` errors instead of eating '--origin').
   135	    if (next === undefined || (next.startsWith('--') && full[next.slice(2)] !== undefined)) {
   136	      return { flags, error: `--${key} requires a value` };
   137	    }
   138	    flags[key] = next;
   139	    i += 1;
   140	  }
   141	  return { flags };
   142	}
   143	
   144	/**
   145	 * Minimal flag parser: `--key value` pairs (value = next token not starting
   146	 * with --), bare `--key` booleans, positionals collected in order.
   147	 * @param {string[]} args
   148	 * @returns {{flags: Record<string, string | boolean>, positionals: string[]}}
   149	 */
   150	export function parseFlags(args) {
   151	  /** @type {Record<string, string | boolean>} */
   152	  const flags = {};
   153	  /** @type {string[]} */
   154	  const positionals = [];
   155	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
   156	    const a = args[i];
   157	    if (a.startsWith('--')) {
   158	      const key = a.slice(2);
   159	      const next = args[i + 1];
   160	      if (next !== undefined && !next.startsWith('--')) {
   161	        flags[key] = next;
   162	        i += 1;
   163	      } else {
   164	        flags[key] = true;
   165	      }
   166	    } else {
   167	      positionals.push(a);
   168	    }
   169	  }
   170	  return { flags, positionals };
   171	}

    66	  const p = worktreePaths(root);
    67

    69	  const existing = io.fs.existsSync(gitignorePath) ? io.fs.readFileSync(gitignorePath, 'utf8') : '';
    70	  const ensured = ensureIgnoreLine(existing, '.worktrees/');

    90	/**
    91	 * Preflight before a child runs in a seat: the worktree is listed, its index
    92	 * is clean, and HEAD sits on the expected branch. Refusals name the failed
    93	 * check and never issue destructive git.
    94	 * @param {string} root @param {{seat: string, branch: string}} target @param {any} io
    95	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true} | {ok: false, check: string, refusal: string}>}
    96	 */
    97	export async function preflightWorktree(root, target, io) {
    98	  const path = worktreePaths(root).seats[target.seat];
    99	  const listed = parseWorktreeList((await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'list', '--porcelain'])).stdout);
   100	  if (!listed.some((e) => e.path === path)) {
   101	    return { ok: false, check: 'missing-worktree', refusal: `worktree ${path} is not listed by git — run setup or self-heal first` };
   102	  }
   103	  const status = (await git(io, path, ['status', '--porcelain'])).stdout.trim();
   104	  if (status.length > 0) {
   105	    return { ok: false, check: 'dirty-index', refusal: `worktree ${path} has a dirty index/working tree — a clean seat is required before a child runs` };
   106	  }
   107	  const head = (await git(io, path, ['rev-parse', '--abbrev-ref', 'HEAD'])).stdout.trim();
   108	  if (head !== target.branch) {
   109	    return { ok: false, check: 'wrong-branch', refusal: `worktree ${path} HEAD is on '${head}', expected branch '${target.branch}'` };
   110	  }
   111	  return { ok: true };
   112	}
   113	
   114	/**
   115	 * Postflight after a child: HEAD still on the expected branch (checked in the
   116	 * seat cwd) and main unmoved from the pre-child capture.
   117	 * @param {string} root @param {{seat: string, branch: string, mainSha: string}} target @param {any} io
   118	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true} | {ok: false, check: string, refusal: string}>}
   119	 */
   120	export async function postflightWorktree(root, target, io) {
   121	  const path = worktreePaths(root).seats[target.seat];
   122	  const head = (await git(io, path, ['rev-parse', '--abbrev-ref', 'HEAD'])).stdout.trim();
   123	  if (head !== target.branch) {
   124	    return { ok: false, check: 'head-drift', refusal: `the child left HEAD on '${head}' instead of the expected branch '${target.branch}'` };
   125	  }
   126	  const main = (await git(io, root, ['rev-parse', 'main'])).stdout.trim();
   127	  if (main !== target.mainSha) {
   128	    return { ok: false, check: 'main-moved', refusal: `main moved under the child (was ${target.mainSha}, now ${main}) — only the merger writes main` };
   129	  }
   130	  return { ok: true };
   131	}
   132	
   133	/**
   134	 * Full-range attribution scan over main..branch — the merge gate. Every
   135	 * commit must be authored AND committed by the sole author, with no forbidden
   136	 * AI-attribution trailer in the body. The range is never windowed.
   137	 * @param {string} root @param {string} branch @param {any} io
   138	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true} | {ok: false, offending: string, reason: string}>}
   139	 */
   140	export async function attributionScan(root, branch, io) {
   141	  const out = (await git(io, root, ['log', `main..${branch}`, `--format=${LOG_FORMAT}`])).stdout;
   142	  for (const record of String(out).split('\x1e')) {
   143	    if (record.trim().length === 0) continue;
   144	    const [sha, an, ae, cn, ce, body = ''] = record.replace(/^\n/, '').split('\x00');
   145	    if (an !== SOLE_AUTHOR.name || ae !== SOLE_AUTHOR.email) {
   146	      return { ok: false, offending: sha, reason: `commit ${sha} has a foreign author identity (${an} <${ae}>) — sole-author is required` };
   147	    }
   148	    if (cn !== SOLE_AUTHOR.name || ce !== SOLE_AUTHOR.email) {
   149	      return { ok: false, offending: sha, reason: `commit ${sha} has a foreign committer identity (${cn} <${ce}>) — sole-author is required` };
   150	    }
   151	    if (TRAILER_PATTERNS.some((re) => re.test(body))) {
   152	      return { ok: false, offending: sha, reason: `commit ${sha} carries a forbidden AI-attribution trailer (co-authored/generated-with/emoji) in its message` };
   153	    }
   154	  }
   155	  return { ok: true };
   156	}
   157	
   158	const mergeLockPath = (/** @type {string} */ root) => `${root}/.handoff/loop/merge.lock`;
   159	
   160	/**
   161	 * Merge an approved subtask branch into main — one guarded transaction:
   162	 * acquire the merge lock (a held lock refuses), gate on the attribution scan,
   163	 * merge at the repo root, abort-and-park on conflict, then ff-only sync both
   164	 * seats. The lock is released on EVERY exit path after acquisition.
   165	 * @param {string} root @param {{branch: string}} input @param {any} io
   166	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true, branch: string} | {ok: false, reason: string}>}
   167	 */
   168	export async function mergeSubtask(root, input, io) {
   169	  const lock = mergeLockPath(root);
   170	  if (io.fs.existsSync(lock)) {
   171	    return { ok: false, reason: `the merge lock at ${lock} is held by another merger — refusing a concurrent merge` };
   172	  }
   173	  ensureDir(io.fs, `${root}/.handoff/loop`);
   174	  atomicWriteJson(io.fs, lock, { host: io.host, pid: io.pid, at: io.now() });
   175	  try {
   176	    const scan = await attributionScan(root, input.branch, io);
   177	    if (scan.ok !== true) return { ok: false, reason: scan.reason };
   178	
   179	    try {
   180	      await git(io, root, ['merge', input.branch]);
   181	    } catch (err) {
   182	      // Conflict (or any merge failure): abort, never auto-resolve — park.
   183	      try {
   184	        await git(io, root, ['merge', '--abort']);
   185	      } catch {
   186	        // Nothing to abort — the failure predates a merge state.
   187	      }
   188	      const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.stdout || /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message || String(err);
   189	      return { ok: false, reason: `merge conflict — aborted and parked for the operator: ${String(msg).split('\n')[0]}` };
   190	    }
   191	
   192	    const seats = worktreePaths(root).seats;
   193	    for (const seat of SEATS) {
   194	      try {
   195	        await git(io, seats[seat], ['merge', '--ff-only', 'main']);
   196	      } catch (err) {
   197	        const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.stderr || /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message || String(err);
   198	        return { ok: false, reason: `worktree wt-${seat} could not fast-forward to main — corruption signal, parked: ${String(msg).split('\n')[0]}` };
   199	      }
   200	    }
   201	    return { ok: true, branch: input.branch };
   202	  } finally {
   203	    try {
   204	      if (io.fs.existsSync(lock)) io.fs.unlinkSync(lock);
   205	    } catch {
   206	      // Best effort — a leaked lock is visible and operator-recoverable.
   207	    }
   208	  }
   209	}

    50	// Any git subcommand that could mutate refs/worktrees/working-tree. On EVERY
    51	// refusal path the full recorded call list must contain none of these.
    52	const DESTRUCTIVE = /\b(branch\s+-[dD]\b|reset\b|clean\b|checkout\b|restore\b|rebase\b|merge\b|worktree\s+remove\b|push\b|commit\b)/;
    53	function assertNoDestructiveGit(git, label) {
    54	  const bad = git.git().filter((c) => DESTRUCTIVE.test(c.argstr));
    55	  assert.deepEqual(bad.map((c) => c.argstr), [], `${label}: no destructive git may be issued on a refusal path`);
    56	}
    57	
    58	/**
    59	 * A recording fake git. `responses` maps an args-substring (longest match wins)
    60	 * to a canned result; { reject:true } models a nonzero git exit. An UNSTUBBED
    61	 * git invocation REJECTS (ENOSTUB) — a test must declare its command surface.
    62	 */
    63	function fakeGit(responses = {}, lockProbe = null) {
    64	  const keys = Object.keys(responses).sort((a, b) => b.length - a.length);
    65	  const calls = /** @type {any[]} */ ([]);
    66	  const fn = (/** @type {string} */ cmd, /** @type {string[]} */ args = [], /** @type {any} */ opts = {}) => {
    67	    const argstr = args.join(' ');
    68	    // Record whether the merge lock is present AT CALL TIME (finding 2): proves
    69	    // mutating git ran under the acquired lock.
    70	    calls.push({ cmd, args, argstr, cwd: opts?.cwd, lockPresent: typeof lockProbe === 'function' ? lockProbe() : undefined });
    71	    if (cmd !== 'git') return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error('spawn ENOENT'), { code: 'ENOENT' }));
    72	    const k = keys.find((key) => argstr.includes(key));
    73	    if (k === undefined) return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error(`unstubbed git: ${argstr}`), { code: 'ENOSTUB' }));
    74	    const r = responses[k];
    75	    if (r?.reject) return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error(r.stderr || 'git failed'), { code: r.code ?? 1, stdout: r.stdout ?? '', stderr: r.stderr ?? '' }));
    76	    return Promise.resolve({ stdout: r?.stdout ?? '', stderr: r?.stderr ?? '' });
    77	  };
    78	  fn.calls = calls;
    79	  fn.git = () => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git');
    80	  fn.issued = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re) => calls.some((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr));
    81	  fn.withCwd = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re, /** @type {string} */ cwd) => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr) && c.cwd === cwd);
    82	  fn.matching = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re) => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr));
    83	  return fn;
    84	}

    89	function mergeRepo(responses = {}, files = {}) {
    90	  const io = makeIo({
    91	    now: '2026-07-19T00:00:00.000Z',
    92	    host: 'wt-host',
    93	    pid: 4242,
    94	    startTime: 111000,
    95	    files: { '/repo/.git/HEAD': 'ref: refs/heads/main\n', ...files },
    96	  });
    97	  const git = fakeGit(responses, () => io.fs.existsSync(MERGE_LOCK));
    98	  io.execFile = git;
    99	  io.__git = git;
   100	  return { io, git };
   101	}

   103	// git worktree list --porcelain output for the given entries.
   104	function porcelain(entries) {
   105	  return entries.map((e) => `worktree ${e.path}\nHEAD ${e.sha ?? MAIN_SHA}\nbranch refs/heads/${e.branch}\n`).join('\n');
   106	}
   107	
   108	function makeRepo({ git, files = {} } = {}) {
   109	  const io = makeIo({
   110	    now: '2026-07-19T00:00:00.000Z',
   111	    host: 'wt-host',
   112	    pid: 4242,
   113	    startTime: 111000,
   114	    files: { '/repo/.git/HEAD': 'ref: refs/heads/main\n', ...files },
   115	  });
   116	  if (git) io.execFile = git;
   117	  io.__git = git;
   118	  return io;
   119	}

   125	// Field order MUST mirror LOG_FORMAT (sha, author-name, author-email,
   126	// committer-name, committer-email, body); records RS(\x1e)-terminated as %x1e does.
   127	const REC = (sha, an, ae, cn, ce, body) => [sha, an, ae, cn, ce, body].join('\x00');
   128	const logStdout = (...recs) => recs.map((r) => r + '\x1e').join('');

   411	    const { mergeSubtask } = M();

   413	      [`log ${RANGE}`]: { stdout: cleanLog },
   414	      [`merge ${BRANCH}`]: { reject: true, code: 1, stdout: 'CONFLICT (content): Merge conflict in src/x.js\nAutomatic merge failed' },

   418	    assert.equal(r.ok, false);
   419	    // The conflicting merge ran at the repo root, under the acquired lock.

   421	    assert.equal(merges.length, 1, 'exactly one merge attempt');
   422	    assert.equal(merges[0].cwd, '/repo', 'the merge ran at the repo root');
   423	    assert.equal(merges[0].lockPresent, true, 'the merge ran under the acquired lock');
   424	    // The abort also ran at the repo root, still under the lock.
   425	    const aborts = git.matching(/^merge --abort$/);
   426	    assert.equal(aborts.length, 1, 'the conflicted merge is aborted exactly once');
   427	    assert.equal(aborts[0].cwd, '/repo', 'the abort runs at the repo root (where the merge happened)');
   428	    assert.equal(aborts[0].lockPresent, true, 'the abort runs under the acquired lock');
   429	    assert.match(String(r.reason), /conflict|park/i, 'the result is a park-shaped refusal');
   430	    assert.ok(!git.issued(/commit|-X (ours|theirs)|checkout --theirs/), 'no auto-resolution was attempted');
   431	    assert.ok(!io.fs.existsSync(MERGE_LOCK), 'the merge lock is released after the conflict abort');
   432	  });

    15	// Every review seat is read-only — writers are the implementer-side roles only.
    16	const REVIEWER_ROLES = new Set(['plan-reviewer', 'test-verifier', 'subtask-reviewer', 'final-reviewer-a', 'final-reviewer-b']);
    17	
    18	// AGENTS.md §9: children commit solely as the repo owner, zero AI attribution.
    19	const GIT_IDENTITY = Object.freeze({
    20	  GIT_AUTHOR_NAME: 'SakshamUboweja',
    21	  GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL: 'ssakshamu@gmail.com',
    22	  GIT_COMMITTER_NAME: 'SakshamUboweja',
    23	  GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL: 'ssakshamu@gmail.com',
    24	});
    25	
    26	/**
    27	 * Assemble the spawn contract for a role child. Reviewers are read-only on
    28	 * every platform; every child is marked supervised (its own baton hooks
    29	 * no-op), carries the sole-author git identity, and has stdin CLOSED at
    30	 * spawn (the codex-exec stdin-hang field failure).
    31	 * @param {{platform: string, role: string, model: string, effort?: string | null, mode?: string}} assignment
    32	 * @param {string} prompt
    33	 * @param {{root: string}} opts
    34	 * @returns {{command: string, args: string[], env: Record<string, string>, stdio: any[]}}
    35	 */
    36	export function buildChildArgv(assignment, prompt, opts) {
    37	  const readOnly = REVIEWER_ROLES.has(assignment.role);
    38	  const env = { BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD: '1', ...GIT_IDENTITY };
    39	  const stdio = ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'];
    40	
    41	  if (assignment.platform === 'claude-code') {
    42	    const args = ['-p', prompt];
    43	    if (readOnly) {
    44	      args.push('--permission-mode', 'default', '--allowedTools', 'Read,Grep,Glob');
    45	    } else {
    46	      args.push('--permission-mode', 'acceptEdits', '--allowedTools', 'Read,Grep,Glob,Write,Edit,Bash');
    47	    }
    48	    return { command: 'claude', args, env, stdio };
    49	  }
    50	
    51	  // codex (and the default shape for exec-style CLIs).
    52	  const args = ['exec', '-C', opts.root, '-s', readOnly ? 'read-only' : 'workspace-write', '--model', assignment.model];
    53	  if (assignment.effort) args.push('-c', `model_reasoning_effort=${assignment.effort}`);
    54	  args.push(prompt);
    55	  return { command: 'codex', args, env, stdio };
    56	}

    36	function version() {
    37	  const pkgPath = join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..', '..', 'package.json');
    38	  return JSON.parse(readFileSync(pkgPath, 'utf8')).version;
    39	}

    41	const USAGE = `usage: baton <command> [options]
    42	
    43	commands:
    44	  ${COMMANDS.join(', ')}
    45	
    46	global options:
    47	  --version   print version
    48	  --json      machine-readable output: one compact JSON envelope on stdout
    49	`;
    50	
    51	/**
    52	 * The --json contract: exactly one compact envelope line on stdout, nothing else.
    53	 * @param {any} io
    54	 * @param {{ok: boolean, data?: any, warnings?: any[], error?: {code: string, msg: string} | null}} env
    55	 */
    56	function emitEnvelope(io, env) {
    57	  const full = { ok: env.ok, data: env.data ?? null, warnings: env.warnings ?? [], error: env.error ?? null };
    58	  io.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(full) + '\n');
    59	}
    60	
    61	/**
    62	 * @param {string[]} argv process args after the binary
    63	 * @param {any} io injected {cwd, stdout, stderr}
    64	 * @returns {number | Promise<number>} exit code
    65	 */
    66	export function run(argv, io) {
    67	  const args = argv.filter((a) => a !== '');
    68	  // Global --json intent is parsed BEFORE dispatch (gate-2 iter-2 M6): a
    69	  // top-level `--json` with no command, or --help/--version, must still emit
    70	  // exactly one envelope rather than raw text.
    71	  const jsonWanted = args.includes('--json');
    72	  const cmdIndex = args.findIndex((a) => !a.startsWith('--'));
    73	  const cmd = cmdIndex === -1 ? undefined : args[cmdIndex];
    74	
    75	  if (cmd === undefined) {
    76	    // No command — just flags (or nothing).
    77	    if (args.includes('--version')) {
    78	      if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { version: version() } });
    79	      else io.stdout.write(version() + '\n');
    80	      return 0;
    81	    }
    82	    if (args.includes('--help')) {
    83	      if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { usage: USAGE.trim() } });
    84	      else io.stdout.write(USAGE);
    85	      return 0;
    86	    }
    87	    // Bare invocation (or a lone --json): a usage error.
    88	    if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: 'no command given' } });
    89	    else io.stdout.write(USAGE);
    90	    return 2;
    91	  }
    92	
    93	  // All flags (before and after the command token) reach the command; only the
    94	  // command token itself is stripped, so a leading `--json` isn't lost.
    95	  const rest = args.filter((_, i) => i !== cmdIndex);
    96	
    97	  // Usage errors honor the --json envelope contract too (gate-2 fix 11 + M6:
    98	  // global --json counts, wherever it sits).
    99	  const wantsJson = jsonWanted;
   100	  if (cmd === 'wrap') {
   101	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: 'baton wrap is reserved for a future release (PTY supervisor); not available in v1' } });
   102	    else io.stderr.write(`baton wrap is reserved for a future release (PTY supervisor); not available in v1.\n${USAGE}`);
   103	    return 2;
   104	  }
   105	  if (!COMMANDS.includes(cmd)) {
   106	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: `unknown command '${cmd}'` } });
   107	    else io.stderr.write(`baton: unknown command '${cmd}'\n${USAGE}`);
   108	    return 2;
   109	  }
   110	
   111	  if (rest.includes('--help')) {
   112	    // Subcommand help honors the global --json envelope too (iter-3 F8): with
   113	    // --json it must emit exactly one envelope, never raw usage text.
   114	    const usage = `usage: baton ${cmd} [options]\n\nSee README for the ${cmd} contract.`;
   115	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { usage } });
   116	    else io.stdout.write(`${usage}\n`);
   117	    return 0;
   118	  }
   119	
   120	  if (cmd === 'status') return cmdStatus(rest, io);
   121	  if (cmd === 'detect') return cmdDetect(rest, io);
   122	  if (cmd === 'remap') return cmdRemap(rest, io);
   123	  if (cmd === 'checkpoint') return cmdCheckpoint(rest, io);
   124	  if (cmd === 'finalize') return cmdFinalize(rest, io);
   125	  if (cmd === 'receive') return cmdReceive(rest, io);

VERDICT: BLOCKED
FINDINGS:
1. [high] Subtask-2 writer alternation is under-pinned. The test collects all `wb-model` calls and checks `w2[0]` for `-C WT_B`, but the subtask-1 reviewer is also expected to be `wb-model` in `WT_B`, so a wrong implementation can satisfy the “subtask 2 writer” assertion with the reviewer; only the first `wa-model` call is asserted `workspace-write` ([pipeline-run.test.mjs:265](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:265), [pipeline-run.test.mjs:270](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:270), [pipeline-run.test.mjs:271](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:271), [pipeline-run.test.mjs:285](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:285)). Fix: filter writer calls by `-s workspace-write` and assert exactly two writers in order: `wa-model`/`WT_A`/subtask `t1`, then `wb-model`/`WT_B`/subtask `t2`.

2. [high] Reviewer coverage only pins subtask 1 and does not prove the child is under the `subtask-reviewer` role. The plan requires seats to swap every subtask ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:216](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:216)), but the test only finds one read-only `wb-model` reviewer in `WT_B` ([pipeline-run.test.mjs:277](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:277), [pipeline-run.test.mjs:285](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:285)). Since read-only is derived from `assignment.role` in `buildChildArgv`, argv alone does not distinguish `subtask-reviewer` from another reviewer role ([children.mjs:15](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/loop/children.mjs:15), [children.mjs:36](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/loop/children.mjs:36)). Fix: assert both reviewer calls in order, including `t1` reviewer `wb-model`/`WT_B`/read-only and `t2` reviewer `wa-model`/`WT_A`/read-only, and assert the prompt/log label includes `subtask-reviewer`.

3. [high] The 5-cap test does not prove “no 6th spawn.” It queues a 6th writer/reviewer pair but only asserts read-only review count `<= 5`; a wrong implementation could spawn a 6th writer and still pass that assertion ([pipeline-run.test.mjs:334](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:334), [pipeline-run.test.mjs:341](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:341), [pipeline-run.test.mjs:349](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:349)). Fix: assert `runner.calls.length === 10`, exactly five writer spawns and five reviewer spawns, no merger calls, no `f6` prompt consumption, and persisted `LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED`.

4. [medium] “Merger child never issues git” is not falsifiable in this suite. The fake runner only records argv and returns a verdict; it cannot observe git issued inside a real child ([pipeline-run.test.mjs:137](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:137), [pipeline-run.test.mjs:148](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:148)). The current assertion proves one supervisor merge for `t1` ran at `/repo` under the lock, but does not constrain the merger child sandbox or every merge call ([pipeline-run.test.mjs:301](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:301), [pipeline-run.test.mjs:306](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:306), [pipeline-run.test.mjs:309](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:309)). Fix: assert the merger gate child is read-only or otherwise non-git-capable, and assert every `merge baton/` and `merge --ff-only main` call has the supervisor cwd/lock shape from `mergeSubtask`.

5. [medium] `loop.json` subtask item validation is missing. The contract requires `subtasks [{id, title}]`, but the precondition tests only cover missing and empty `subtasks` ([pipeline-run.test.mjs:64](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:64), [pipeline-run.test.mjs:230](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:230)). Fix: add malformed-item cases such as missing `id`, missing `title`, non-string fields, and duplicate/unsafe ids if rejected by the intended branch naming rules; each should exit 2 with zero spawns.

6. [low] Some usage-error zero-spawn cases are unasserted. The stray positional and unknown-flag test only checks exit 2 on throwaway repos, while the contract says usage errors exit 2 with zero spawns ([pipeline-run.test.mjs:203](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:203), [pipeline-run.test.mjs:205](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:205)). Fix: keep the `io` for each case and assert `io.__runner.calls.length === 0`.

7. [low] Attribution-park only forbids `merge baton/wt-a/`, not any subtask merge. The test title says no merge git call lands, but the assertion is anchored to `wt-a` only ([pipeline-run.test.mjs:385](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:385), [pipeline-run.test.mjs:393](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:393)). Fix: assert `io.__git.matching(/^merge baton\\//).length === 0` after attribution failure.
